# Bash tests of setup, install, manage and services in TypeScript

*Sub-spec J1b of the `large`-tier ticket #1231, row J1b of the architect decomposition
(<https://github.com/crewrig/crewrig/issues/1231#issuecomment-5857477069>). Parent
spec: `specs/0215-shell-to-typescript-migration.md` (requirement 13, row J, step (e) of
requirement 8), under the release-branch regime of
`specs/0215-shell-to-typescript-migration.delta-04.md` (requirements 27-38): this
spec-PR and its implementation pull requests target `release/1231-ts-migration`,
"shipped" means merged there, not reached `main`, and the base ref wherever a protocol
names `main` is `origin/release/1231-ts-migration` (requirement 34). Depends on the
sub-specs whose scripts these suites exercise and which shipped green: D
(`specs/0252-windows-service-management.md` and its deltas), F2
(`specs/0255-install-manage-link-typescript.md` and its deltas) and F1
(`specs/0256-setup-entry-points-typescript.md` and its deltas), and on row H
(`specs/0258-skill-extension-bundled-scripts-typescript.md`). The model is the sibling
row J1a (`specs/0260-hook-ops-tests-typescript.md` and its delta-01). Siblings of the
same wave: J1a (#1340, done), J2 (#1343), J3 (#1342, `specs/0261-e2e-harness-probes-typescript.md`)
and the final removal J4 (#1344).*

## Intent

A maintainer who changes a setup entry point, an installer, the component manager, the
MemPalace service scripts or the doctor gets the same regression coverage as before, from
tests that run with `node --test` on Linux, macOS and Windows, with no Bash test file left
for these scripts. A reviewer can see, suite by suite, which behaviour each old Bash
assertion checked, which new test checks it now, and evidence that a broken implementation
which turned the old suite red turns the new suite red too. A lockfile change on the
release branch or on `main` no longer breaks the setup goldens (issue #1614, delivered
first).

### What a migrated suite preserves

4. **Replacement per suite.** Every suite of requirement 1 SHALL have the replacement
   suite and the assertion mapping given in *Suite sections* below. Each replacement file
   SHALL stay under 300 lines, a shared helper lives under `scripts/tests/lib/` and every
   new file is TypeScript (parent requirements 1-3 and 11). Where the *Suite sections*
   report that an existing `.test.ts` already covers a group, the mapping names that test
   and the PLAN verifies by grep that it asserts the exact string before the Bash group is
   dropped (the *VERIFY AT PLAN TIME* notes).
5. **No assertion is lost.** Every case and group of a Bash suite SHALL appear in the
   mapping of its suite exactly once, with the replacement test that checks it and the
   strings or values that the replacement SHALL keep verbatim. A replacement MAY assert
   more than the Bash suite did, and SHALL NOT assert less, except for the entries listed
   under *Changed or dropped assertions* of its suite section, each with its justification.
6. **Sourced-library suites.** Many suites source `scripts/lib/common.sh`,
   `scripts/lib/gemini-settings.sh`, `scripts/lib/tls-exec.sh` or a function shim and call
   shell functions in-process. Under the sourced-library exception of parent requirement 13:
   (a) where the TypeScript twin of the function has its own black-box suite on the release
   branch, the Bash group retires and the mapping names, per assertion, the substitute test;
   (b) where no such test exists, the group SHALL be ported to a TypeScript test that spawns
   `bash` and sources the library, in its own file, POSIX-only (`skip` on `win32` with a stated
   reason), carrying the header comment `Retires with <library> (row J4)`, and the J4 ledger
   (#1344) SHALL list it so that J4 deletes it in the same diff as the function. A replacement
   SHALL call the TypeScript entry, not a shim that J3 or J4 later deletes, whenever the
   behaviour is the same. This reading of the boundary between J1b and J4 is confirmed with
   the coordinator before the first pull request opens.
7. **Shell-only properties.** An assertion that checks a property only a shell file has
   (a `bash -e` contract, a `bash -n` syntax check, an `EXIT` trap that restores a mutated
   tracked file, a nested `node --test` wrapper) SHALL be removed with its Bash suite and
   listed under *Changed or dropped assertions* (parent requirement 13).
8. **Mutations on a copy.** A mutation case SHALL mutate a temporary copy of the implementation
   files, never a tracked file in place, SHALL fail, not skip, when the text it needs to mutate
   is absent, and a mutated copy SHALL contain only the files that git tracks and a real copy
   of `js-yaml` and `argparse`, never `node_modules` and `dist` picked up from the host.
9. **Non-vacuity.** A replacement that iterates over fixtures SHALL assert a floor on the
   number of fixtures it found. A prerequisite that the CI runner image provides (a real
   `jq`, `packaging` for Python, `node:sqlite`, a pipx `mempalace` environment) SHALL make the
   test fail when the environment variable `CI` is set and skip with a stated reason otherwise.
10. **Hermeticity.** A replacement SHALL run with a scratch `HOME` and `USERPROFILE`, SHALL
    delete `CI` and any proxy variable that it does not mean to test from the environment of a
    child process that runs a partial copy of the repository, SHALL use a free local port for any
    server and a stub service directory (`CREWRIG_TEST_SERVICE_BIN_DIR`) for any service
    manager, SHALL stub `gh`, SHALL set `CREWRIG_USAGE_ROOT` and a closed port or stub daemon
    with a scratch token whenever a hook could reach the real MemPalace daemon, and SHALL write
    nothing under the real `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot` or `~/.crewrig`. No test SHALL
    register a real LaunchAgent, systemd unit or scheduled task. Each file that runs a setup or
    an installer SHALL use the real-home guard (`scripts/tests/lib/real-home-guard.ts`) and the
    proofs of requirement 14 check the real home directories afterwards.
11. **Entry under test.** A replacement SHALL run the same entry the Bash suite ran, the `.sh`
    shim where the Bash suite exercised the shim, and the TypeScript entry directly through
    `process.execPath` where the Bash suite only needed the behaviour. The shim SHALL stay covered
    by at least one test per script that this row's suites reach through it.
12. **Behaviour of the scripts is frozen.** No pull request of this row SHALL change the
    behaviour of a script under test. A defect found while porting a suite SHALL be reported to
    the coordinator and the replacement SHALL assert the current behaviour with a comment naming
    the report. The suite `test-component-tier-resolution.sh` case 20 (requirement R19, the twelve
    Taskfile entry points) is already red at the base commit and listed `KNOWN_RED` by
    `install-oracle-mutation.test.ts`: the replacement asserts the current Taskfile and the
    pull request reports the case.

### The setup goldens (issue #1614)

13. **Decoupling first.** Before any suite pull request that touches the setup goldens, their
    sandbox SHALL stop copying the repository's real `package-lock.json`: it copies a fixed
    fixture lockfile (`scripts/tests/fixtures/setup-golden/package-lock.json.golden`), and the
    stamp hash recorded in the 127 `tree.json.golden` files that record the dependency stamp (of 144) and the 12-hex prefix in the 4
    `deps-step-hit/stdout.golden` files take the fixture's value once. #1614 is delivered as its
    own small ticket flow (one pull request, #1649, base `release/1231-ts-migration`, branch
    `test/1614-decouple-setup-goldens-from-lock`, no spec) and SHALL be merged before the first pull
    request of this row that edits a suite under the setup sandbox. The alternative (normalising the
    hash in the capture) is rejected: it keeps the coupling and needs a normaliser that must stay in
    sync with every capture path.

### Proof that coverage did not regress

14. **Mutation proof.** For each suite, the *Mutation set* of its section lists the mutants of the
    implementation that the proof applies. The pull request that replaces a suite SHALL show, in its
    body, a table with one row per mutant: the Bash suite run at the base commit turns red, the
    replacement turns red, and the unmutated baseline is green for both. A mutant that the Bash suite
    caught and the replacement does not catch blocks the pull request. Mutants named in the sections
    are proposals: the PLAN confirms the exact text of each against the file (*VERIFY AT PLAN TIME*).
15. **Two environments.** A replacement that spawns Git, a local server, `bash`, `jq` or a Python
    interpreter SHALL be run in an Ubuntu 24.04 container that mirrors the CI runner (Bash 5.2, `jq` 1.7,
    Node 24, GNU `tar` and `gzip` on the hermetic path) in two images, one with Node under `/usr/local/bin`
    and one under `/opt/hostedtoolcache/node/<version>/x64/bin`, before the pull request opens, with `CI=true`
    reproduced for any CI-aware skip, and a golden value that contains a host-specific string (a Node path,
    a timestamp, a random token, a port) SHALL be normalised. The real home directories are checked after
    each proof.
16. **Mapping audit.** Each pull request SHALL carry, in its body, the count of Bash cases removed and the
    count of replacement tests that cover them, and the reviewer seat SHALL be able to reconcile the two
    from the mapping of this spec.

### Wiring and deletion

17. **Deletion and allowlist.** The pull request that replaces a suite SHALL delete the Bash file, remove its
    line from `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` and keep `check-test-wiring` and `check-test-strays` green (parent
    requirements 10 and 12). Fixtures that only the deleted suite read and that are tracked `.sh` or `.js`
    files SHALL be ported or stored with a `.golden` suffix so that the ratchet baseline does not grow.
18. **CI wiring.** Each CI job that ran a deleted suite SHALL run its replacement through `node --test`,
    inside the stray-scan wrapper wherever the Bash invocation had it, in the same file and under the same job
    id, and the path filters of the job SHALL name the replacement files (several `--key-files` globs such as
    `test-mempalace-*.sh` need extending). The new suites SHALL join the existing TypeScript jobs, on Linux and
    on `windows-latest` (parent requirement 17), and the generated files SHALL be regenerated from
    `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` with `bash scripts/build-ci.sh` rather than edited.
19. **Windows.** The files of a suite that need no POSIX tool SHALL run on `windows-latest`; a file that needs
    `bash`, a POSIX permission bit, a symbolic link or a Python wrapper SHALL skip on `win32` with a stated
    reason, and the Windows job SHALL name that skip so that it is visible. A Windows job SHALL NOT pick
    `System32\bash.exe`, SHALL resolve interpreters from the parent `PATH` before narrowing the child `PATH`,
    and SHALL split test files under 300 lines. A POSIX golden needs a separator and root normaliser or a
    POSIX-only skip. Windows git rejects `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=<os.devNull>`: use `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`.
20. **Static reads.** Every file that names a deleted suite SHALL be retargeted in the pull request that deletes
    the suite, and the *Static reads and wiring* of each section lists them. The files that read a Bash suite
    as data, and that break when it is deleted, are enumerated in the PLAN before the first pull request and
    are retargeted in their own pull request BEFORE the pull request that deletes the suite when the diff
    would otherwise exceed review size; they include `install-oracle-mutation.test.ts`,
    `build-components-fixture-closure.test.ts` (a new inline `js-yaml argparse` literal in a suite SHALL be
    registered in its `LITERALS`), `lib/setup-declarations-consumers.ts`, `setup-bash-triggers.test.ts`,
    `install-ci-wiring.test.ts`, `extension-ci-wiring.test.ts`, `build-components-ci-wiring.test.ts` and
    `fixtures/install/old-invocation-allowlist.txt`. The stale comment of
    `scripts/tests/test-mcp-daemon.sh` line 367 (`# next to the dynamic argv-stub test
    (test-mempalace-transcript-hook.sh) that`, naming a suite that J1a deleted) is carried into the
    replacement static-guards test with the name `mempalace-transcript-hook.test.ts`. Edits to files of a
    sibling row, or of `test-check-bash32-portability.sh` (row J2) which names a J1b suite, go to the
    coordinator as an `escalation` instead of being made here, unless the coordinator authorises them in
    writing.

### Delivery

21. **Pull requests.** The implementation SHALL ship as five pull requests into the release branch, in the
    grouping of the *Decision record*, after #1614 (requirement 13). Every implementation branch but the
    last SHALL be named `test/1341-<slug>`, and the last SHALL flip this spec and its deltas to
    `status: implemented`. Each pull request SHALL be rebased on the release tip, SHALL have green CI
    including `ratchet`, `lint-typescript` and the Windows jobs, and SHALL get an `APPROVE` verdict from a
    cold reviewer seat before a merge is asked.

### Suite sections

For each suite: the replacement files, the mapping from each Bash case or group to the replacement test,
the assertions changed or dropped, the mutation set, the platform and the wiring. Case labels are those of
the Bash suite, so that `git log -p` of the suite at the base commit remains the reference. Items marked
*VERIFY AT PLAN TIME* are resolved in the PLAN before the first pull request opens.

#### Setup suites that source `common.sh` (pull request A)

##### `scripts/tests/test-setup-catalogue-picker.sh` (275 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** Groups G1..G5 in file order: G1 = L99-129 (empty catalogue, section 1), G2 = L131-173 (declined pick with stub `fzf`, section 2), G3 = L175-193 (normal pick, section 3), G4 = L195-257 (declaration parity, section 4), G5 = L259-275 (TypeScript smoke through `run_ts_behaviour`, section 5). Overlap check done (`ls scripts/tests`, grep): `scripts/tests/setup-catalogue.test.ts` (172 lines) already covers the TypeScript twin `pickCatalogueEntry` for the empty, declined and normal pick including the shell's messages; `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-catalogue-picker.test.ts` (64 lines) already covers G4 (three categories, `cancel=decline`, `<config/...>` options, `.selected_<category>` marker, per CLI); `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-behaviour-a.test.ts` (258 lines, pattern `catalogue-picker`, 4 tests, Linux sandbox) already is G5. Only G1-G3 (the SHELL function, still shipped until J4) lack a Node-side test. Nothing in G4/G5 is re-implemented.

| replacement file | what it holds | est. lines |
|---|---|---|

- **Assertion mapping.**

| label | behaviour asserted | replacement::test intent | MUST survive |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | `pick_catalogue_entry <empty dir> team` with `fzf` removed from PATH: rc 0, stdout empty, stderr has no `command not found` (fzf never invoked), stderr contains `No team catalogue entries found` | common-catalogue::empty catalogue never reaches fzf (PATH built from the real PATH minus every dir holding `fzf`, as Bash does) | rc 0; empty stdout; substring `No team catalogue entries found`; no `command not found` |
| G2 | one-entry dir (`backend.md`, body `# fixture`), stub `fzf` that writes stdin to `$FZF_STDIN_CAPTURE` and exits 1, category `expertise`: rc 0 (the `\|\| true`), stdout empty, capture matches neither `^\*$` nor `\*\.md`, capture equals exactly `backend`, stderr contains `No expertise selected` | common-catalogue::declined pick is neutralised, real candidate only | rc 0; capture == `backend`; no `*` / `*.md`; substring `No expertise selected` |
| G3 | stub `fzf` that drains stdin and prints `backend`: rc 0, stdout `backend` | common-catalogue::golden pick returns the basename | stdout exactly `backend` |
| G4 | per cli in claude, gemini, copilot, antigravity: declaration non-empty, `rules.pick-order` present, `step N: rules-selection` present, sorted order is `expertise,level,team`, per category `prompt.catalogue.<c>.cancel=decline`, `prompt.catalogue.<c>.options` matches `<config/...>`, `rules.marker.<c>` ends `/.selected_<c>` | covered by existing `setup-retarget-catalogue-picker.test.ts`; no new test | cancel value `decline`, marker suffix, three-category set, vacuity guards |
| G5 | four CLIs: empty team catalogue + declined expertise + picked level exits 0, prints both notices, level marker and level rule land, stale team/expertise markers removed; exactly 4 passing tests | covered by `setup-retarget-behaviour-a.test.ts` pattern `catalogue-picker` | count 4 (assert the pattern still selects 4 tests via a `describe` count, not a wrapper) |

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** The `grep -qi "command not found"` proxy for "fzf never invoked" becomes a stub `fzf` that writes a sentinel file on any call; the test asserts the sentinel is absent (stronger, same property). The `STRIPPED_PATH` construction is replaced by a PATH holding only a stub dir plus the minimal `bash` dir. The Linux-only prerequisite probe (`ts_sandbox_prereq_missing`) and `run_ts_behaviour` output scraping are dropped: the underlying tests already carry `skip` and `node --test` is the runner. Nothing else dropped.
- **Mutation set.** (1) `scripts/lib/common.sh` `pick_catalogue_entry`: delete the `if [ "${#candidates[@]}" -eq 0 ]` block (L534-537; message at L535; function starts L525, `shopt -s nullglob` L528, `candidates+=(...)` L530, `|| true` L541, `No $category_label selected` L543) -> G1 (fzf reached, sentinel present). (2) same file: remove `|| true` after the `fzf` pipeline -> G2 (rc non-zero). (3) same file: `No $category_label selected` to `Nothing selected` -> G2. (4) same file: `candidates+=("$(basename "$f" .md)")` to `candidates+=("$f")` -> G2 capture and G3. (5) same file: remove `shopt -s nullglob` -> G1 (literal `*.md` fed). (6) `scripts/lib/setup/catalogue.ts`: change the declined-pick message `No <label> selected` text -> existing `setup-catalogue.test.ts` and G5. (7) the cli descriptor `scripts/lib/setup/cli-claude.ts`: flip a catalogue prompt `cancel` from `decline` to `abort` -> G4. Mutate temp copies of git-tracked files only; the new file runs `common.sh` through `bashLibs` with `REPO` pointing at the temp copy (parameterise by an `CREWRIG_REPO_UNDER_TEST` env var, default the checkout).
- **Platform.** `setup-common-catalogue-picker.test.ts`: POSIX-only (skip on win32: Bash library, stub `fzf` shell script). Existing files unchanged (retarget file all platforms; behaviour-a Linux sandbox as today).

No `Taskfile.yml` or `docs/` reference found.

Path comparison uses the realpath helper; on win32 the command form is `hookCommandLine` (forward slashes) - reuse `hookCommandLine` from `scripts/lib/hook-command.ts` rather than hard-coding, or skip G2/G3 equality on win32 with the reason "Windows command form pinned by `scripts/tests/lib/windows-setup-proof.ts` (not a test file)" (decide in PLAN).

Transcript" steps run through `ci-cache-guard.sh`); `scripts/tests/lib/setup-declarations-consumers.ts` L62 (suite name string `test-setup-claude-transcript.sh (r3 #4)` in the declaration-consumer table, with `hooks.env-patch` key: retarget to the new file name); comments in `specs/0256-setup-entry-points-typescript.md` L213, `specs/0248-...md` L483, `specs/0260-...md` L72, `specs/0170-efficient-test-strays-guard.md` L46, L49 (specs are history, leave). ##### `scripts/tests/test-setup-copilot-transcript.sh` (193 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** Groups: G1 = L63-101 manifest schema (valid JSON; `hooks` object; `version` is `1`; every event key matches `^[a-z][a-zA-Z0-9]*$`; every entry has `type == "command"` and `command` or `bash`; the `info:` line is not an assertion), G2 = L106-116 workspace settings (`config/copilot/settings.json.template` and `.github/copilot/settings.json`: `hooks` is an object and `version == 1`), G3 = L123-148 render + jq replay, patched valid with object hooks, G4 = L150-165 guard command and five events, G5 = L167-189 token absence and spec 0211 R2 (asserted on the SOURCE manifest). Common with Claude/Gemini: G3 (render), G5 token/usage checks. Existing coverage: `setup-steps-copilot.test.ts` and `setup-steps-hooks.test.ts` reference `settings.json.template` / the copilot manifest through the flow, but no existing `.test.ts` asserts the camelCase/version/entry-shape contract (verify in PLAN with `grep -n "camel" scripts/tests/*.test.ts`).
- **Design flag (self-referential replay).** G3/G4 replay a jq program copied into the Bash test, not the shipped one: the "patched" commands asserted for the five events (`$ENVP bash "<hook>"`) are the output of the test's own jq, so the assertion proves nothing about setup. The shipped behaviour is the TypeScript `merge-session-recording-hooks` / copilot step (`scripts/lib/setup/copilot*.ts`, covered by `setup-steps-copilot.test.ts`). The replacement keeps the guard-command check (real render output, G4 first leg) and replaces the five-event leg by an assertion against the real user-level result: run the entry that applies the env patch (`usage-capture-optin.ts merge-session-recording-hooks copilot ...` or the setup step, to be pinned in PLAN) over a scratch config and assert the five events carry the hook path and `MEMPALACE_TRANSCRIPT_ENABLED=1`. If PLAN finds `setup-steps-copilot.test.ts` already asserts it, drop the leg and say so explicitly (a documented dropped assertion, not a silent one).

| `scripts/tests/setup-transcript-copilot.test.ts` | G1-G5 on the shared rig | ~130 |

| G2 | template and committed settings: `.hooks` is an object and `.version == 1` | copilot::workspace settings, two tests | both files; names `config/copilot/settings.json.template`, `.github/copilot/settings.json` |
| G3 | render succeeds; patched output valid JSON with object hooks | copilot::render | object hooks |
| G4 | `preToolUse[0].command == node "<realpath>/worktree-git-guard.ts"` without `MEMPALACE_TRANSCRIPT_ENABLED`; events `sessionStart`, `userPromptSubmitted`, `postToolUse`, `agentStop`, `sessionEnd` each carry the hook path and `MEMPALACE_TRANSCRIPT_ENABLED=1` | copilot::guard command; copilot::five events (see design flag) | five camelCase names; env prefix `MEMPALACE_TRANSCRIPT_ENABLED=1`; `preToolUse` arm is a pass-through (v1-F2) |

(5) `.github/copilot/settings.json`: `"version": 1` to `2` -> G2. (6) `scripts/hook-wiring.ts` guard render for copilot: emit the `.sh` path -> G4. (7) same file: on a non-object target, print `Transcript hooks deployed` and return 0 -> G15. (8) same file: leave `<target>.tmp` behind on failure -> G15. (9) `scripts/hook-wiring.ts` transcript antigravity-merge: also rewrite the command of a foreign-named hook -> G17. (10) `hooks/mempalace-transcript.ts`: drop the `{}` acknowledgement on the opted-out path -> G22; drop the `(terminationReason)` suffix -> G25. (11) `hooks/antigravity-statusline-shim.ts`: propagate the prior command's exit status -> G28 Case C. (12) `scripts/hook-wiring.ts statusline rewrite`: write the marker before the settings, or skip dropping `previousInstalledStatusLineCommand` -> G27 crash legs. All on a temp copy of tracked files (`scripts/tests/lib/mutation-copy.ts`).
- **Isolation.** Real-state exposure of the Bash suite: (a) the deploy helper is given explicit scratch paths, so no real `~/.gemini` is written, but `render` is run without a scratch HOME or CREWRIG_USAGE_ROOT (reads the real usage opt-in state); (b) G23 and G24 invoke the hook with `MEMPALACE_TRANSCRIPT_ENABLED=1` and NO `MEMPALACE_MCP_HOST/PORT`, no `MEMPALACE_DAEMON_TOKEN_FILE`, no `NO_PROXY`: a valid payload (G24) can therefore reach the real default daemon port with the real `~/.mempalace` token under the real HOME - the replacement MUST pass the stub port (or a closed port from `closedPort()`), a scratch token file, and scratch HOME and USERPROFILE on every hook run; (c) G22 with `ENABLED=0` touches nothing; (d) G27 uses scratch settings and marker paths (`$TMP_ROOT/antigravity-cli-settings.json`), keep them, plus scratch HOME; (e) G28 sets `CREWRIG_USAGE_ROOT` scratch, never the real `~/.crewrig/usage`. Port: the stub daemon reports a free port through `--port-file`. No launchd/systemd/schtasks, no gh. Never touch real `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot`.
- **Platform.** `setup-transcript-antigravity-manifest.test.ts`: all platforms. `setup-transcript-antigravity-deploy.test.ts`: all platforms when it calls `antigravity-hooks.ts` directly (mode 0600 assertions use a visible `skip` with reason "NTFS has no POSIX mode bits"; `setup-antigravity-hooks.test.ts` L140 uses a silent `process.platform !== "win32"` guard, so it is not the precedent). `mempalace-transcript-antigravity-payload.test.ts`: POSIX-only for the `bash hooks/mempalace-transcript.sh` leg (skip on win32: Bash shim; the entry equivalent runs in `mempalace-transcript-windows.test.ts`), with an all-platform leg that spawns `node hooks/mempalace-transcript.ts antigravity-cli Stop` directly. G28 shim leg: POSIX-only (Bash shim), entry leg already in `usage-capture-windows.test.ts`.
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1701, L1733-1744, L1751, L1783, L2940, L2994; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2173, L2206, L2247, L2280, L3809, L3864; `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L194; `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1449, L1496, L1502-1535; `.github/workflows/usage-capture.yml` L41, L96 (path trigger and the run step; this workflow is the usage-capture family, coordinate with row J1a's edit of the same file); `docs/cli-matrix.md` L51 (row 6 test-suite list names the suite; `docs/cli-matrix.md` MUST be updated in the same diff per AGENTS.md CLI Matrix Maintenance); `docs/agent-profile-migration.md` L102; `scripts/tests/lib/setup-declarations-consumers.ts` L69 (suite name string with keys `hooks.unused-copy`, `home.cli`); comments naming the suite in `scripts/tests/setup-antigravity-statusline.test.ts` L3, `scripts/tests/setup-antigravity-hooks.test.ts` L3, `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-antigravity-transcript.test.ts` L1 (rewrite to point at the new files, or leave as history if the file is deleted: those headers say "the expectations are those the Bash suite ... pins"); specs `0247 ...delta-02.md` L18, L56, L220, L250, `0247 ...delta-03.md` L541, `0243-usage-capture-hooks-typescript.md` L391, `0256` L215, `0248` L485, `0260` L72 (history). The four `ci-cache-guard` key-file lists (`.gitlab-ci.yml` L1733-1744, `build.yml` L1496) are shared with the mempalace and system-context suites; edit them by removing only the four names.

##### `scripts/tests/test-setup-usage-capture-optin.sh` (1982 lines)

- **What the Bash suite is.** An in-process regression suite for the usage-capture opt-in of Claude Code, Gemini CLI and Copilot CLI (spec 0211, #1174). It `source`s `scripts/lib/common.sh` and `scripts/lib/usage-capture-optin.sh` and calls the library functions (`usage_capture_{state,paths,footprint,reinject,enable,disclose,keep,remove,apply,rewrite,abs,fragment,require_node_floor}`, `merge_session_recording_hooks`, `backup_file`) directly, with a test-owned jq oracle (`JQ_DEFS`: `is_c1`, `legacy_path`, `is_capture`, `handlers`, `noncapture_view`) written independently of the library, and 11 fixtures under `scripts/tests/fixtures/setup-usage-capture/` (`{claude,gemini,copilot}-coupled`, `-operator`, `-operator-stripped`, `claude-nohooks`, `gemini-capture-only`, `gemini-capture-only-stripped`; placeholders `__CAPTURE_ABS__`, `__HOOK_TARGET__`, `__GUARD_ABS__`). Pass/fail is counted per `ok`/`bad`; it prints `PASS: n  FAIL: n`.
- **Entry and shim exercised.** Shim: `scripts/lib/usage-capture-optin.sh` (one `node` process per function call, `node -r scripts/lib/node-floor-guard.js`, side channel `--result` for `SR_TRANSCRIPT_WIRED`/`SR_ALL_HOOKS_DISABLED`). Entry: `scripts/usage-capture-optin.ts` (subcommands per `scripts/tests/lib/usage-entry-rig.ts`). Setups `scripts/setup-{claude,gemini,copilot}-interactive.sh` are shims of `.ts` entries (row F1) and are only reached through `setup-retarget-behaviour-a.test.ts` (G31) and the declaration printer (G2, G30).
- **Overlap with existing TypeScript suites (checked with `ls scripts/tests` and a read of each header; no assertion below duplicates these).**
  - `setup-usage-capture.test.ts` (295 lines), `setup-usage-capture-keep.test.ts` (280), `setup-lib-usage-entry.test.ts` (235), `setup-twins-differential-usage.test.ts` (121): all DIFFERENTIAL (shell function vs TypeScript twin on two copies of one input), so they assert agreement, not the absolute values the Bash suite asserts. They have no oracle of their own and die with the shell shim. The new suites assert absolute behaviour; they MUST NOT be built on the shim and MUST NOT depend on these files.
  - `setup-usage-capture-state.test.ts` (197) runs `recognition-corpus.json` against the TS readers; `hook-recognition.test.ts` (221) runs the same corpus against `hook-recognition.ts`. Hence G32 is covered (see its row).
  - `setup-usage-capture-rank.test.ts` (140): dedup ranking live > unresolvable > vanished, unit level; G27 is the end-to-end counterpart and stays.
  - `setup-usage-capture-wire-path.test.ts` (37): Windows wire path; G26 is the POSIX refusal counterpart.
  - `setup-lib-usage-entry-form.test.ts` (84): entry form, refusals, below-floor leaves file system unchanged; covers half of G37 (see row).
  - `setup-lib-shim-usage.test.ts` (252): shim-only contracts (`set -u`, no `exit`, no `trap`, stdin untouched, `uname` MINGW makes keep/apply take the PowerShell form, `--result` whitelist). Covers G20 and the `uname` leg of G36.
  - `setup-steps-usage.test.ts` (193): the `usage-capture` flow step through a fake Spawner against golden stdout; asserts printed lines and Copilot bytes, not the library matrix.
  - `setup-retarget-usage-order.test.ts` (42): declaration order and prompts of the three CLIs (this is the already-retargeted half of G2 and G30). `setup-retarget-behaviour-a.test.ts` (258) holds the 4 `usage-capture` tests the Bash suite runs at §5 (G31).
  - `setup-declarations.test.ts` (273) pins the declaration printer by golden and `setup-declarations-consumers.ts` L49 lists this suite as a consumer (keys `step:session-recording`, `step:usage-capture`, `prompts.usage-capture`, `prompt.usage-capture.options`, `prompt.usage-capture-keep.options`, `prompt.usage-capture.header`, `usage-capture.strategy`, `usage-capture.target`).
  - `setup-golden-{claude,gemini,copilot,antigravity}.test.ts` carry the golden cells `usage-capture-{absent-yes,absent-no,installed-keep,installed-remove}`, `transcript-optin-*`, `copilot/usage-capture-unreadable-hooks`; they stay as the pin for the §2/§4 retirements.
  - `usage-capture-shim.test.ts` / `usage-capture-hook.test.ts` / `usage-capture-windows.test.ts` cover `hooks/usage-capture.sh` and `.ts` execution; G17 and the run leg of G25 only need ONE successful registered-command run each, kept here because they prove the REGISTERED command string works.
  - `hook-guard-corpus.test.ts`, `hook-transcript-corpus.test.ts`: corpus runners for the guard and transcript predicates (G38 target).
- **Replacement suite.** Group labels G1..G38 are this inventory's numbering of the Bash suite's test groups in file order. The `[ ok/FAIL ]` count of the Bash run is large (per-CLI loops ×3), so the count of replacement `test()` blocks is not 1:1: each loop becomes a `for cli of CLIS` table-driven test, one `test` per (cli, case) so that a regression names its CLI. Estimated 120-140 generated tests.

| replacement file | what it holds | est. lines |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/tests/lib/optin-oracle.ts` (shared, new) | TypeScript port of the jq oracle, written independently of `scripts/lib/hook-recognition.ts` and `scripts/lib/setup/usage-capture-state.ts` (it re-implements `is_c1` as a regexp over the same grammar and `legacy_path` with `fs.existsSync`): `handlers(cfg)`, `isCapture`, `captureCount/Cmds/Events`, `totalCapture`, `noncaptureView`, `stripCaptureNoPrune`; per-CLI tables `cliEvents`, `cliTag`, `expectedCmd`, `legacyCmd`, `homeConfig`, `nonR5Event`; `materialize(fixture, dest, cap?)` (placeholder substitution into a copy, never in place); `patchedManifest`; `addCmds`, `initCfg`, `emptyGroups`; `fileMode`, `backups(cfg)`, `jsonEq`; `runOptin(sub, args, {env, cwd})` over `node scripts/usage-capture-optin.ts` (reuse `scripts/tests/lib/usage-entry-rig.ts` `node`/`ENTRY`, do not duplicate); `scratch()` returning `{home, usageRoot, tmp}` with HOME and USERPROFILE both pointing into a `fs.mkdtempSync` dir | ~190 |

  Every file stays under 300 lines; if `keep` nears the cap, split G33-G36 into `usage-capture-optin-rewrite.test.ts` (~130). G20 and G38 have no file (see mapping). G31's four behavioural tests stay in `setup-retarget-behaviour-a.test.ts` and are NOT re-run by a nested `node --test` as the Bash `run_ts_behaviour` does (that wrapper, its `CI` skip rule and its prerequisite probe `ts_sandbox_prereq_missing` are Bash plumbing; the file runs on its own in CI, so no pass-count scraping is needed; if the vacuity guard is wanted, assert from a directory scan of the file that at least 4 tests contain the name pattern `usage-capture`).

- **Isolation.**
  - Real HOME paths touched by the Bash suite: none by design; it exports `HOME="$TMP_ROOT/home"` and `CREWRIG_USAGE_ROOT="$TMP_ROOT/usage"`. It writes only `$HOME/.claude/settings.json`, `$HOME/.gemini/settings.json`, `$HOME/.copilot/hooks/copilot-transcript-hooks.json` (via `home_config`) inside the scratch HOME, and asserts at G30 that no `usage-capture.sh` landed under `$HOME/.{claude,gemini,copilot}/hooks`. The replacement MUST set BOTH `HOME` and `USERPROFILE` (spec 0260's rule; the Windows `os.homedir()` reads `USERPROFILE`) to a `fs.mkdtempSync(os.tmpdir())` directory, set `CREWRIG_USAGE_ROOT`, and pass `env` explicitly to every spawn (`hermetic-env.ts` / `cleanEnv()` from `lib/worktree-fixtures.ts`, `NODE_OPTIONS` and `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT` removed). `scripts/tests/lib/real-home-guard.ts` MUST be used in an `after()` to assert that the real `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot` trees are byte-unchanged (hash of the listing). Never touch real `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot`.
  - OS services: none (no launchd, systemd, schtasks). Network: none, no port. Bash suite says "no network; no `fzf`; no interactive script runs".
  - External commands: `jq` (oracle and fixtures; the replacement DROPS jq entirely, so the `command -v jq` FATAL goes away and the Windows runner no longer needs it), `git` (G11 only: temp repository plus `git worktree add --detach`; keep real git, with `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR` deleted from the child env and `-c user.name=t -c user.email=t@example.invalid -c commit.gpgsign=false`), `node` (G17, G25 run: real node via symlink in a private PATH dir; G37: a fake `node`), `stat`, `sort`, `find`, `cmp` (replaced by `fs` calls), `/bin/sh -c "$cmd"` (G17, G25: the registered command string is executed verbatim through the platform shell, POSIX only).
  - `umask 022` is pinned by the Bash suite so that 0644 sources really are 0644 (G19, G21, G29); the replacement calls `process.umask(0o022)` in a `before()` and restores it in `after()` (or `chmodSync` each fixture explicitly, preferred, because `process.umask` is process-wide and `node --test` runs files in separate processes anyway).
  - The Bash suite's `trap 'rm -rf "$TMP_ROOT"' EXIT` becomes `after(() => fs.rmSync(root, {recursive: true, force: true}))`, with a `try/finally` in the git worktree test (the linked worktree registers itself in the main repository's `.git/worktrees`, inside the scratch tree, so deleting the scratch root is sufficient).
  - Stub gh: not needed (no forge call). Stub service dir `CREWRIG_TEST_SERVICE_BIN_DIR`: not needed (no service). Free port: not needed.

- **Changed or dropped assertions.**
  - Dropped: G20 (Bash `-e` contract of the shim; covered by `setup-lib-shim-usage.test.ts`), the `uname` MINGW stub of G36 (replaced by `--platform win32`, same decision, shim part covered by `setup-lib-shim-usage.test.ts`), G38's Bash twin (covered), the `jq`/`git` FATAL preflights (`jq` is no longer needed; `node:test` runs on Node 24 floor so the `command -v node` FATAL becomes a hard `before()` assertion, never a skip), the Bash `run_ts_behaviour` wrapper (nested `node --test`, pass-count scraping and the `CI` skip rule: replaced by the direct file plus the count guard of G31).
  - Changed: `cmp -s` becomes `Buffer.equals` on `fs.readFileSync`; `stat -c %a`/`-f %Lp` becomes `fs.statSync(f).mode & 0o777` (`lstatSync` for links); `compgen -G "$cfg.bak.*"` becomes a `fs.readdirSync(dirname)` prefix filter (bash 3.2 glob semantics are not needed); `jq -S` equality becomes a deep-sorted `JSON.stringify` (key-sorted, array order significant, as `jq -S` keeps array order); the oracle moves from jq programs to the TS port in `lib/optin-oracle.ts` and is cross-checked against the shared corpus (G32).
  - The `.sh` shim paths: expected commands keep BOTH forms (`node "<...>.ts"` direct, `bash "<...>.sh"` legacy) exactly as `expected_cmd`/`legacy_cmd` do.
  - Placeholders `__CAPTURE_ABS__`, `__HOOK_TARGET__`, `__GUARD_ABS__` are substituted into scratch copies at test time; no machine path is committed (keep this property; `check-no-machine-paths` runs over the fixtures).
  - No assertion is weakened. Count: the Bash suite does not print a fixed `PASS:` total in the source, so the plan cannot cite a number to preserve; the replacement fixes instead a non-vacuity guard per loop (`for cli of CLIS` asserts `CLIS.length === 3`, event lists per CLI non-empty).

- **Mutation set** (each applied to a temp copy of the git-tracked file, never the working tree; the Bash suite catches each, the new group must go red too).
  1. `scripts/lib/hook-recognition.ts` L80: in the grouped-form regexp, drop the trailing `$` of `(?<post>${args})$` so a command with extra arguments reads as capture -> G22 (`--extra` look-alike: state must be `absent`), G32.
  2. `scripts/lib/hook-recognition.ts` L80: drop the leading `^` -> G22 (`echo hi; bash "<CAPTURE_ABS>" ...`), G9 (leading/trailing compound), G22 N1 `and`/`semi`.
  3. `scripts/lib/hook-recognition.ts` L103: remove `\\x00-\\x1f\\x7f\"';&|<>()$` ... from the legacy `pathClass` (accept `;` and `&`) -> G22 N1 `semi`/`and`, `and-existing`.
  4. `scripts/lib/setup/backup.ts` L50: replace `if (st.isFile() && mine) fs.chmodSync(old, 0o600);` with an empty statement (no narrowing) -> G21; and remove `st.isFile()` (follow links) -> G29.
  5. `scripts/lib/setup/backup.ts` L71: `mode: 0o600` -> `mode: 0o644` on the new backup -> G21 first assertion.
  6. `scripts/lib/setup/json-secure.ts` L134: delete `fs.chmodSync(file, 0o600);` (or `0o600` -> `0o644` at L104) -> G3 (mode), G19, G29.
  7. `scripts/lib/setup/usage-capture-keep.ts` L133: delete the `out("  Usage capture re-pointed ...")` line, or make `keep` re-point a still-resolving path -> G11, G10 (`re-pointed` absent), G23, G35.
  8. `scripts/lib/setup/usage-capture-rank.ts`: swap the rank so `vanished` beats `live` (first of the best rank) -> G27 (live command must survive), G10 duplicate collapse.
  9. `scripts/lib/setup/usage-capture-fragment.ts` L66: change the refusal predicate so `$` is allowed in the checkout path, or the message `cannot be wired safely` to another wording -> G26 (the `$` and symlink legs).
  10. `scripts/lib/setup/usage-capture-write.ts` L88-89: write the new config at `0o644` instead of `0o600` -> G3 file-mode assertion; and in the same file remove the temp-file cleanup -> G28 (stray `*.tmp*`).
  11. `scripts/lib/setup/usage-capture-cli.ts`: in `remove`, prune every empty container including operator-owned ones, or make `remove` skip `hooks` pruning -> G14 (`SessionEnd` still present/removed), G13.
  12. `scripts/lib/node-floor-guard.js` (used through `node -r`): make the guard return `ok` for `v20.19.5` -> G37.
  Both the Bash suite and the new suites catch 1-12 because every assertion is on the written bytes; mutants 4 and 5 are the weakest links in a 0600-masking umask, hence the explicit 0644 starting files of G19/G21/G29. Mutate a temp copy via `scripts/tests/lib/mutation-copy.ts` (reuse; it already copies git-tracked files only) and run the single replacement file against it with the module root pointed at the copy.

- **Platform.**
  - `lib/optin-oracle.ts`: all platforms (no `jq`).
  - `usage-capture-optin-fragments.test.ts`: all platforms.
  - `usage-capture-optin-enable.test.ts`: all platforms except G17, the run leg of G25 (`/bin/sh -c`, restricted `/usr/bin:/bin` PATH) and G26's names with `"`/newline: `skip` on win32 with reason "registered command is run through a POSIX shell; Windows wiring is covered by `usage-capture-windows.test.ts` and `setup-usage-capture-wire-path.test.ts`".

  - `usage-capture-optin-keep.test.ts`: all platforms; the linked-worktree leg (G11) needs `git` and is skipped (with reason) when `git` is absent, but FAILS under `CI`; the `/opt/...` strings are inert text, not paths.

#### Install, manage and components (pull request C)

##### `scripts/tests/test-component-tier-resolution.sh` (1466 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** Group labels `G1`..`G27` are this inventory's numbering of the Bash suite's cases in file order. The Bash case label is kept in the table so `git log -p` at the base commit stays the reference. The Bash suite builds a throwaway repository per case (copy of `scripts/`, `Taskfile.yml`, `package.json`, empty `.git`, real copy of `js-yaml` and `argparse`), seeds components, runs the real shims with `HOME` redirected, and asserts the landing zones. The replacement keeps that black-box shape through `bash <fixture>/scripts/<name>.sh` (the shim is the real user path), reusing `scripts/tests/lib/build-fixture-tree.ts` (`createFixtureTree`, `productionClosure`, which already stages `js-yaml` and `argparse` and replaces the `STAGED_NODE_PACKAGES` literal) and `scripts/tests/lib/hermetic-env.ts` (`createHermeticEnv`: scratch HOME and USERPROFILE). The seed helpers are the only new helper.

- **Isolation.** Real paths touched by the Bash suite: only `$WORK/*` fixtures and `HOME=$WORK/<root>/cli-home*`, plus read of the real repo `scripts/`, `Taskfile.yml`, `package.json`, `node_modules/{js-yaml,argparse}`. Run-time external commands: `git` (G19, G27), `jq` (G8), `node`, `bash`, `go-task` (optional, G25), `cp`, `diff`. No OS service, no network port, no `gh`. The replacement sets HOME and USERPROFILE to a scratch directory per fixture via `createHermeticEnv` (and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` unset), uses `productionClosure()` for the dependency copy (`cpSync` with `dereference: true`, which is the `cp -RL` rule), never uses `go-task` (fixed driver), replaces `jq` by `JSON.parse`, and asserts the real tree is unchanged (G27). `mktemp -t crewrig-0119.XXXXXX` becomes `fs.mkdtempSync(os.tmpdir(), ...)`; cleanup in `after`.
- **Changed or dropped assertions.** (1) `jq -e` becomes `JSON.parse` property checks (same property). (2) The `STAGED_NODE_PACKAGES` literal and the missing-package NOTE line are dropped: `productionClosure()` replaces them, and `build-components-fixture-closure.test.ts` L26 is retargeted to the new helper in the same diff (it currently greps the Bash text). (3) The `diff -q` `cp`-alias guard of G14 becomes a `fs.copyFileSync` plus byte-equality assert. (4) The bash 3.2 notes (no `declare -A`, no `mapfile`) are dropped, not applicable to TS. (5) `$BASH_VERSION` banner dropped. (6) G13/G15/G16 sub-assertions already held at the unit level are not repeated (named in the table). (7) G25: see reported defect. Nothing else dropped.
- **Mutation set.** All mutants are applied to a temp copy of git-tracked files (staged fixture), never the working tree. They are the seven already in `install-oracle-mutation.test.ts` (ids 1 gemini staging root, 2 org tier dropped, 3 antigravity skills dest, 4 antigravity customization root, 5 migration guard `placed.length > 99` and 6 migration called with no names, both AGY-only in `scripts/lib/manage/overlay-loop.ts`, 7 empty helper; spec mutants (5) to (10) of the tier list are new), which must be rewritten to run the new `.test.ts` files (they read `FAIL:` lines from Bash output today) plus new ones: (1) `scripts/lib/manage/descriptors.ts`: `staged("skills", ".gemini/skills", ".gemini/skills", "gemini")` to `".gemini/skills-x"` for the first path -> G24 red. - **Isolation.** Bash suite touches: `$SANDBOX` (`mktemp -d`), `GEMINI_HOME`, the real child `scripts/install-extension.sh` (writes under `GEMINI_HOME`, also `HOME`? In the Bash suite `HOME` is NOT redirected: a real install to `$HOME/.gemini/extensions` is avoided only because `GEMINI_HOME` overrides it; check `scripts/lib/install/extension.ts` for any other `$HOME` write). The replacement sets HOME and USERPROFILE to the scratch directory in addition to `GEMINI_HOME`, so a defect cannot reach the real `~/.gemini`, `~/.claude`, `~/.copilot`. No service, no port. PATH is built with `createHermeticEnv`/`createInstallSandbox({ deps: ..., clis: [] })` (no CLI stubs, so every target is skipped except Gemini through `GEMINI_HOME`).
- **Changed or dropped assertions.** G1 to G3 are not re-implemented (named coverage in `install-all.test.ts`); G1's literal and G4's summary substring are added to those tests if absent (no new test). The `echo | grep -Fq` pipefail note is dropped (no pipes). `/usr/bin:/bin` PATH: kept on POSIX; the test is skipped on win32.
- **Mutation set.** (1) `scripts/lib/install/all.ts` L179 `target(s) were skipped` reworded -> G4 red. (2) `all.ts`: change `[SKIPPED]` padding to two spaces for Claude -> G5 red. (3) `all.ts` L122 `not found in extensions/` reworded -> `install-all.test.ts` red (G3, existing coverage). (4) `all.ts`: do not exit 1 when every target is skipped -> G4 red. (5) `Taskfile.yml`: rename `install-extension-all:` to `install-extensions-all:` -> G6 red. (6) `Taskfile.yml`: drop the `[DEPRECATION] 'task link-extensions' is deprecated` line -> G7 red. (7) `scripts/install-extension-all.sh`: fall through when `node` is absent (exit 0) -> `install-shim.test.ts` (existing) red, not this suite. Mutate a temp copy only.
- **Platform.** POSIX-only for G4 and G5 (`/usr/bin:/bin` PATH, `.sh` shim, symlinks). G6 and G7 all platforms (static reads; keep them in a separate `describe` without the POSIX skip).
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1385, L1393, L1454; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L1846, L1908, L1974; `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L167; `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1192 (path trigger), L1272 (`ci-cache-guard.sh --stray-scan -- bash scripts/tests/test-install-extension-all.sh`); `scripts/tests/fixtures/install/old-invocation-allowlist.txt` L46; `scripts/tests/install-ci-wiring.test.ts` L55; `scripts/tests/extension-ci-wiring.test.ts` L61; `scripts/tests/install-all.test.ts` L2 (comment: "the shell's own oracle is test-install-extension-all.sh", reword in the same diff); specs `0177` L58, `0254` L499, `0255` L468, `0255.delta-01` L139 (history). The new `.test.ts` is added where the Bash suite is listed (stray-scan step, capability `commands`, path filters).

##### `scripts/tests/test-install-claude-plugin-marketplace.sh` (207 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** Entry `scripts/install-claude-plugin.ts` (`scripts/lib/install/claude-plugin.ts`, upsert in `scripts/lib/install/marketplace.ts`, build in the Claude plugin builder), through the shim `scripts/install-claude-plugin.sh`. The Bash suite keeps a REAL `jq` ("it is the script under test that becomes jq-free, not its oracle"); the replacement parses the manifest with `JSON.parse` and does not need `jq`, so the Bash `command -v jq` FATAL and the "must stay real" rule are dropped. Overlap check: `marketplace.test.ts` (L46 `upsertMarketplace`, L102, L117) unit-tests the merge/upsert; `extension-plugin-claude.test.ts` (L30-L142) tests the plugin tree builder; neither runs the install entry end to end against a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` with a stub `claude`, which is what this suite does. Not duplicated: the new file only drives the entry.

| replacement file | what it holds | est. lines |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/tests/install-claude-plugin-marketplace.test.ts` | G1 to G4 end to end, real extension `extensions/core/hello-world`, stub `claude` | ~150 |

- **Assertion mapping.**

| label | behaviour asserted | replacement::test intent | MUST survive |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 (L152-171, Case 1, R4 + branch-switch) | fresh `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, install `hello-world` with stub `claude` on PATH (`exit 0`): exit 0; manifest exists at `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/local-marketplace/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`; it lists a plugin named `hello-world` (`.plugins[].name`); no `dist-claude-plugin` directory exists anywhere under the repo (`find -not -path '*/.git/*'`) | e2e::out-of-tree location, four assertions as four tests or one test with four labelled asserts | exit 0 with stub; the out-of-tree path literal; the repo-wide absence scan (skip `.git` and `node_modules` in the walk, scan in the real repo before and after rather than assert empty, so a developer's pre-existing dir does not mask it: compare the set) |
| G2 (L173-190, Case 2, R1+R2) | manifest pre-seeded (`seed_manifest`: `name` `seeded-local`, `owner.name` `crewrig contributors`, plugin `other-ext` with description `Synthetic prior install`, author `seed`, source `./other-ext`); install `hello-world`: exit 0; both `other-ext` and `hello-world` are listed | e2e::coexistence | seed JSON verbatim; both names |
| G3 (L192-208, Case 3, R5) | install `hello-world` twice into one config dir: both exit 0, exactly ONE `hello-world` entry (`length == 1`) | e2e::idempotent | count 1 |
| G4 (L210-222, Case 4) | pre-seeded manifest, `definitely-not-an-extension`: exit non-zero and `shasum` of the manifest identical before and after (byte-for-byte) | e2e::failed install leaves registry intact | non-zero (any), byte equality via `node:crypto` sha256 of the file |

- **Isolation.** Bash suite touches: `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=$TMP_ROOT/cfg.XXXXXX` per case (so the real `~/.claude` is not written), a stub `claude` (`exit 0`) prepended to PATH, the real repo tree read-only (`extensions/core/hello-world`, builder), and, as a hazard, the REAL `$HOME` (unredirected) for anything the build step might write. The replacement additionally redirects HOME and USERPROFILE to the scratch directory, keeps `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` explicit, builds the stub `claude` with `writeStub` from `hermetic-env.ts` (recording argv, so the new test can also assert `claude plugin marketplace add <marketHome> --scope user` is called, an observable `claude-plugin.ts` L38 makes available; this is an ADDED assertion, listed below), and never touches the real `~/.claude`. No service, port, or network.
- **Changed or dropped assertions.** (1) Dropped: `command -v jq` precondition and the "jq stays REAL" rule (the implementation is jq-free since spec 0254/0255 and the replacement parses with `JSON.parse`; recorded here because spec 0255 requirement 26 said the oracle keeps the real `jq`). (2) Added, not in Bash: the stub records its argv, and G1 asserts the `marketplace add` call carries the out-of-tree home; this strengthens R4 and is optional (drop if the stub-argv helper is not wanted). (3) `shasum` replaced by `node:crypto`. (4) `find "$REPO_DIR" -name dist-claude-plugin` replaced by a bounded recursive walk. Nothing else dropped.
- **Mutation set.** (1) `scripts/lib/install/claude-plugin.ts` L30: `path.join(config, "local-marketplace")` to `path.join(config, "local-marketplace-x")` -> G1 red. (2) same file L27-29: ignore `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (always `~/.claude`) -> G1 and every group red or the real-home guard fires (the mutant must run under scratch HOME, so it lands in the scratch home, and the manifest-at-`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` assertion goes red). (3) `scripts/lib/install/marketplace.ts` L52: remove the filter (`return plugins`) -> G3 red (two entries). (4) `marketplace.ts` upsert: replace instead of append (drop existing plugins) -> G2 red. (5) `claude-plugin.ts`: write the manifest before the extension lookup resolves (build to a repo-local `dist-claude-plugin`) -> G1 red (stray dir). (6) `claude-plugin.ts`: on an unknown extension, truncate the manifest before returning 1 -> G4 red. (7) `claude-plugin.ts` L21: return 0 instead of 1 when `prepared === null` -> G4 red. All on a temp copy of tracked files.
- **Platform.** POSIX-only: stub `claude` is a `#!/bin/bash` shim on PATH and the entry is reached through `bash scripts/install-claude-plugin.sh`. A node-direct leg (`node scripts/install-claude-plugin.ts` with a `claude.cmd` stub) belongs to the Windows install proof (`scripts/tests/lib/windows-install-proof.ts`) and is out of scope here.
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1382, L1390, L1451; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L1843, L1905, L1971; `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L166; `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1189 (path trigger), L1263 (stray-scan step); `scripts/tests/install-ci-wiring.test.ts` L56; `scripts/tests/extension-ci-wiring.test.ts` L59; `scripts/tests/test-build-extension.sh` L725 (comment citing this suite's `jq` use, reword); specs `0045` (cases map to its scenarios), `0254` L499, `0255` L469, `0255.delta-01` L139 (history). The old suite's header cites spec 0045 scenarios; the new file's header cites them identically.

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** `ts_sandbox_prereq_missing` / `run_ts_behaviour` pass-count wrapper dropped (nested `node --test` of another suite); replaced by a title-existence assertion. The `diff -r` calls become a recursive comparison with `fs.readdirSync(..., {recursive:true})` plus `Buffer.equals`. `find -maxdepth 1 -name '*.md'` stays non-recursive.
- **Mutation set.** (1) `artifacts/core/system-context/<one>.md`: truncate to empty -> G1. (2) `60-tools.md`: change one `~/.crewrig/system-context/x.md` token to a missing file -> G2. (3) add an unreferenced `orphan.md` to the store -> G3. (4) `common.sh install_dir`: drop `rm -rf "$target"` (stale files survive) -> G4/G5. (5) `install_dir`: always `ln -sfn` -> G4. (6) `install_dir`: clear only `[ -d ]` but not `[ -L ]` -> G5 switch. (7) `60-tools.md`: rename the `## Retrieving the system-context store` heading -> G6. (8) `common.sh print_store_access_guidance`: remove the `--add-dir` line -> G6 (and `setup-store-guidance.test.ts`). (9) TS twin `installDir` (`scripts/lib/setup/files.ts`): copy without the dot-files (`cp -R src/*`) -> the `setup-files.test.ts` equivalent.
- **Platform.** `system-context-store.test.ts`: all platforms (reads, no spawn). `system-context-install-dir-common-sh.test.ts`: POSIX-only (bash library, symlinks).
- **Isolation.** The Bash suite writes only under `mktemp -d` (`$TMP_ROOT`); it does not touch `~/.crewrig`, `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot` and needs no network or service; its headless per-CLI probe is deliberately excluded (header L16-18; results in `docs/research/system-context-sandbox-probe.md`). Replacement: `mkdtemp`; HOME and USERPROFILE set to scratch for the bash port so a mutant that expands `~` cannot reach the real store; read `artifacts/core/...` in place (git-tracked, read-only).
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1448 (path trigger), L1496, L1502-L1535 (key files list); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1700, L1733-L1744, L1750, L1782; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2172, L2205, L2246, L2298 (`--stray-scan`); `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L198; `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-gemini-copilot-run.test.ts` L7 (comment); `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-others.test.ts` L9 (comment "test-system-context-store.sh (4)"); `scripts/tests/lib/setup-declarations-consumers.ts` L112 (`suite: "scripts/tests/test-system-context-store.sh (r3 #12)"`); `specs/0256-setup-entry-points-typescript.md` L219; `specs/0260-hook-ops-tests-typescript.md` L73; `scripts/tests/setup-store-guidance.test.ts` (diffs against `common.sh`: must stay green until J3).

##### `scripts/tests/test-setup-gemini-settings-merge.sh` (917 lines)

- **Overlap check (ls scripts/tests, grep).** Existing TS suites that already touch this behaviour, so the plan does not duplicate them:
  - `setup-gemini-settings.test.ts` (255): differential `mergeGeminiSettings` vs `gemini_settings_write` over a matrix (includes `1/**/2` and `01` rows, org keys sorted for warnings), "the differential is not vacuous", commented-settings decision, seed-not-object failure, `readAndMerge`, custom wrapper.
  - `setup-gemini-settings-write.test.ts` (295): fresh home, operator keys + seeds + python registers mempalace, comments warning + backup, invalid JSON replaced, directory-as-target returns 1, unreadable template returns 1 naming the backup, org fold, backup printed before change, 0600 off win32, stale `<target>.tmp` returns 2, `explainGeminiRc`.
  - `setup-gemini-settings-write-diff.test.ts` (104) and `setup-gemini-settings-rc.test.ts` (49): Bash-vs-TS differential and the rc 2 chmod leg.
  - `setup-retarget-others.test.ts` L81, L95 (declaration order, "gemini: org-mcp-read precedes gemini-settings-write", "the declared order puts the settings write first") and `setup-retarget-gemini-copilot-run.test.ts` L68 ("Gemini setup (TypeScript entry): settings merge and decline messages", 1 test in its first block (L69); re-count at the PR base): these ARE the replacement of §13 already; this plan adds nothing for the second half of §13. G13 first half (sub-step order `org-mcp-read` < `gemini-settings-write` < `ensure-mempalace-http`) is already asserted by `setup-retarget-others.test.ts` L81 and L95: drop; keep only `mcp` before `session-recording` and `usage-capture` (`cli-gemini.ts` L28-33) if absent.
  - What is NOT covered today and is therefore new: the inline oracle independent of `gemini_framework_mcp` (R11), seeds/ancestor matrix (§2, §8), the hooks across accept-then-decline runs (§3), the context.fileName union table (§4), precedence warnings (§10), the number-grammar tables 6b/6c, idempotent three-run document equality (§9), the 5 s size bounds (§12), the symlink planted at `<target>.tmp` (11f) and the playwright verbatim entry (§14).
- **Replacement suite.** Group labels `G1`..`G14` are the inventory's numbering of the Bash suite's 14 numbered sections, in file order. Case labels inside a group are the Bash labels (`1a`, `6b`...).

| replacement file | what it holds | est. lines |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/tests/lib/gemini-settings-rig.ts` (shared, new) | `newCase()` (fresh HOME and USERPROFILE under `os.tmpdir()`, creates `.gemini/`, recording `io.out`/`io.err`); the R11 oracle computed from `config/gemini/settings.json` with literals for the reserved entries (`__REPO__`, `__PY__` substituted, NOT derived from the writer); `FRESH_PY`, `FRESH_NOPY`, `ORACLE_MEMPALACE`, `ORACLE_SEQ`; `gsWrite(py, orgNative)` over `geminiSettingsWrite`; `setupRun(py, org, session, capture, http)` composing the TS counterparts in the setup's order (settings write, mempalace HTTP registration, `mergeSessionRecordingHooks` on the manifest from `renderSessionRecordingManifest` patched as the setup does, then `captureState`/`usageCaptureApply`); `expectJson`, `expectMode600`, `expectNoTmp`, `backupAfter`, `expectWarningBackup`; the `COMMENT_WARN` / `INVALID_WARN` constants | ~120 |
| `scripts/tests/setup-gemini-settings-seeds.test.ts` | G1, G2, G4, G5, G8, G10 | ~210 |

- **Isolation.** Real HOME paths touched by the Bash suite: none (it sets `HOME="$TMP_ROOT/caseN"` before sourcing, so `~/.gemini` is the scratch one). No fzf, no daemon, no network (`register_mempalace_mcp` only writes the file; the interpreter `/opt/crewrig-test/bin/python3` is a fake path nothing executes). External commands: `jq` (hard requirement, `FATAL` if absent), `cp` and `jq` stubs on PATH (11b, 11d), `ls -l`, `awk`. Replacement: every case creates `fs.mkdtempSync(os.tmpdir()/crewrig-gsm-)`, passes it as `ctx.home` and sets `HOME` and `USERPROFILE` to it in any child; `jq` is no longer needed (TS merge), so no `jq` skip; no OS service, no port, no stub `gh`. The `real-home-guard.ts` helper (`scripts/tests/lib/real-home-guard.ts`) is used in the rig `after()` to assert `~/.gemini/settings.json` mtime unchanged.
- **Assertion mapping.**

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** (1) `jq` is not needed: oracle and comparisons run on parsed JS values with sorted-key stringify, replacing `jq -Sc`. `FATAL: jq is required` is dropped (no jq in the TS path). (2) `expect_mode600` (`ls -l`) becomes `fs.statSync().mode & 0o777 === 0o600`, skipped on win32 with the reason "POSIX mode bits". (3) G11b "a `cp` that always fails" becomes `mock.method(fs, "writeFileSync")` throwing for the `.bak.` path (the backup is written with `fs.writeFileSync(bak, ..., {flag:"wx", mode:0o600})` in `scripts/lib/setup/backup.ts`; there is no `copyFileSync`) (same technique family as `setup-gemini-settings-rc.test.ts`); the expected sentence is unchanged. (4) G11d "a jq wrapper that fails on `org_min_reserved`" has no TS equivalent (no jq); the rc 2 property is kept through the one remaining TS path that returns 2 with the merged file in place (an org fold that cannot complete: a stale `<T>.tmp` that is a non-empty directory, as in `setup-gemini-settings-write-diff.test.ts` "stale target.tmp directory (rc 2)", or `mock.method(fs,"renameSync")` on the fold write). The ERROR sentence names the stale `<T>.tmp` in the TS port (`... but removing the stale <T>.tmp did not complete.`, existing test L253) rather than the fold sentence: the spec must say which of the two sentences the port pins and flag the deviation. (5) G11e/G11f keep their intent (a pre-existing `<T>.tmp` that is wide-open or a symlink must never receive the secret nor become the target). (6) G12 timing uses `performance.now()` and builds the commented fixture in TS (no `sed`, `seq`, `wc -c`). (7) G13 nested `node --test` wrapper dropped. (8) G4c and G8 literal sentences: assert on the rendered output; the TypeScript code composes `'${key}' in ${target} is not ${what}; it was replaced by the framework's value.` with `what` = `a string or a list of strings` or `an object`, so the full sentence is not a source literal. Nothing else dropped.
- **Mutation set** (each applied to a temp copy of the tracked files through `scripts/tests/lib/mutation-copy.ts` `copyTracked`/`applyMutation`, never in place; the old Bash suite catches the Bash-library mutants, the new suite the TS-module mutants):
  1. `scripts/lib/setup/gemini-settings-merge.ts`: make a seed overwrite an operator `false` (`??` to `||` equivalent where the seed is chosen) -> G2 `2b`. Bash twin: `scripts/lib/gemini-settings.sh` replace the object-preserving `*` operand order -> G2 `2b`.
  2. `gemini-settings-merge.ts`: do not de-duplicate `context.fileName` -> G4 `4a` and G9.
  3. `gemini-settings-merge.ts`: keep the operator's stale `mempalace` entry (drop the reserved replacement) -> G5 `5a` (`STALE-SECRET` survives).
  4. `gemini-settings-merge.ts` (JSONC reader): accept `01` or `+1` (loosen the number grammar) -> G6 `6b`.
  5. `scripts/lib/jsonc.ts`/reader: join tokens across a block comment (`1/**/2` becomes 12) -> G6 `6`.
  6. `gemini-settings.ts`: skip the backup call (or move it after the rename) -> G4 `4c`, G5, G11a, G11b.
  7. `gemini-settings.ts`: drop the removal of the stale `<target>.tmp` before the folds -> G11f (symlink target receives the secret).
  8. `scripts/lib/setup/json-secure.ts`: write at the default 0644 instead of 0600 -> every `expect_mode600` (G1, G2, G6, G9, G11).
  9. `gemini-settings-merge.ts`: org entries lose to operator entries (swap merge order) -> G10 (`org-acme`).
  10. `gemini-settings-merge.ts` or the reader: quadratic comment scan (re-scan from the start for every string) -> G12b/G12c exceed 5 s.
- **Platform.** `setup-gemini-settings-seeds`, `-jsonc`, `-runs`: all platforms, with the 0600 assertions skipped on win32 ("POSIX mode bits"); `-failure`: all platforms except 11e/11f and the 0600 leg, which are POSIX-only (`skip` on win32: a symlink needs privileges and the 0644 pre-state has no meaning). The rig has no POSIX tool dependency (no jq, awk, ls, cp stubs).
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1802 (`bash scripts/ci-cache-guard.sh --stray-scan -- bash scripts/tests/test-setup-gemini-settings-merge.sh`); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L2003; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2557; `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L189; `scripts/tests/lib/setup-declarations-consumers.ts` L148 (`suite: "scripts/tests/test-setup-gemini-settings-merge.sh (r3 #16)"`, a registry of declaration consumers: the entry must be repointed to the new file or the consumer registry loses the "r3 #16" retarget); `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-others.test.ts` L8 (comment); `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-gemini-copilot-run.test.ts` L4 (comment naming section 13); specs: `specs/0256-setup-entry-points-typescript.md` L220, `specs/0260-hook-ops-tests-typescript.md` L72, `specs/0247-mempalace-transcript-hook-typescript.md` L565, `specs/0247-...delta-01.md` L479 and L536, `specs/0247-...delta-02.md` L142 and L214, `specs/0248-worktree-git-guard-typescript.md` L485 (specs are not edited by this ticket; list only). The same four CI files are checked by `scripts/tests/setup-bash-triggers.test.ts` (L256-260 pins the mcp-merge line only) and by `scripts/check-stray-scan-wiring.ts`; no glob covers `scripts/tests/*.test.ts`: jobs list TypeScript tests by explicit name in `node --test` commands, so every new test file is added by name to the command and to the `paths:` and cache `files:` lists of the owning job (capability `setup` for gemini-settings and tls); `check-test-wiring.ts` and `check-stray-scan-wiring.ts` (R17a, R17b) police only `test-*.sh`, so an unlisted file never runs and nothing goes red.

##### `scripts/tests/test-tls-delegation.sh` (200 lines)

- **What it does.** Sources `scripts/lib/tls-delegation.sh` (the function shim over `scripts/tls-delegation.ts`, spec 0256 R33) and drives `detect_custom_tls_context` and `offer_tls_delegation` through the non-interactive `TLS_DELEGATION` path in a scratch HOME (`mktemp -d`; `HOME` reassigned per case; every TLS/cert variable `unset` by `reset_tls_env`), then runs `bash scripts/lib/tls-exec.sh`, reads setup declarations, and parses `Taskfile.yml`.
- **Overlap check.** `setup-tls-detect.test.ts` (140 lines, `candidateCa`, `detectCustomTlsContext`, includes `UV_NATIVE_TLS`/`UV_SYSTEM_CERTS` as signals, L94-95), `setup-tls-offer.test.ts` (246: consent/decline writes, `UV_SYSTEM_CERTS=true`, `rm <file>` hint), `tls-env-writer.test.ts` (six exports in order, `UV_SYSTEM_CERTS`), `setup-lib-tls-entry.test.ts` and `setup-lib-tls-entry-offer.test.ts` (entry exit codes, `TLS_DELEGATION` bypass, `--result`), and `setup-lib-shim-tls.test.ts` (L5 names this Bash suite as its oracle; shim return codes, stdout, variable round trip) already cover G1, G2, G3 and part of G4 behind the same code. `setup-retarget-others.test.ts` L52 covers G6 ("the TLS offer is invoked by the tls-offer step", per CLI, with `fact()`). `grep -l REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED scripts/tests/*.ts` returns nothing: the R5 invariant, the `tls-exec.sh` wrapper (G5) and the Taskfile `install-mempalace` prefix (G7) have NO other test. Hence one new file.
- **Group inventory (file order).**

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** G1/G6 dropped as covered (named above). G3/G4 move from a sourced Bash function to the real entry with `TLS_DELEGATION_CA`; the shim round trip is already pinned by `setup-lib-shim-tls.test.ts`. G4 gains an existence assertion. The Bash `awk` extraction becomes a JS line scan that must locate `^  install-mempalace:` and its `cmd:` or fail. `skip` for host anchors stays visible.
- **Isolation.** The Bash suite already redirects HOME; the replacement sets HOME and USERPROFILE to a `mkdtemp` dir (`box()` of `lib/tls-entry-run.ts` does so), and removes the host's TLS and proxy variables (`TLS_DELEGATION`, `TLS_DELEGATION_CA`, `CREWRIG_TLS_CA`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE`, `PIP_CERT`, `GIT_SSL_CAINFO`, `CURL_CA_BUNDLE`, `UV_SYSTEM_CERTS`, `UV_NATIVE_TLS`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY`) from the child env; `box()` of `lib/tls-entry-run.ts` builds the env from `PATH`, `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` plus extras only, so no host variable leaks by construction, and a non-vacuity assertion on that key set replaces the delete list. Never reads or writes the real `~/.crewrig/tls-env.sh`, `~/.zshrc`, `~/.bashrc`. No network, no OS service, no external command beyond `bash`, `printenv`, `echo`, `node`. Add `realHomeGuard()`.
- **Mutation set.** (1) `scripts/lib/tls-env.ts` (writer): emit an extra `export NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0` -> G4 (the Bash suite catches it through the file the shim writes). (2) writer: emit `export UV_NATIVE_TLS=true` -> G3. (3) `scripts/lib/tls-exec.sh`: delete the `. "${_tls_env}"` line -> G5 (marker not propagated). (4) `scripts/lib/tls-exec.sh`: `exec "$@"` -> `exec true` -> G5 (both legs). (5) `scripts/lib/setup/tls-offer.ts`: write the file even when `TLS_DELEGATION=off` -> G2. (6) `Taskfile.yml` `install-mempalace`: remove `bash {{.REPO_DIR}}/scripts/lib/tls-exec.sh` -> G7. (7) `scripts/lib/setup/tls-detect.ts`: drop `UV_SYSTEM_CERTS` from the signal list -> G1 (`setup-tls-detect.test.ts` and the Bash suite). (8) any `scripts/setup-*` declaration (setup declarations module): remove the `tls-offer` step for one CLI -> G6 (`setup-retarget-others.test.ts`).
- **Platform.** `tls-delegation-contract.test.ts`: G4, G2, G3 all platforms (entry-based, no Bash) but G5 is POSIX-only (`skip` on win32: Bash wrapper; Windows uses `tls-exec.ts` installed by setup) and G7 all platforms (file read; the Taskfile cmd is POSIX syntax by design).
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1742 (paths-filter `setup:` list entry `'scripts/tests/test-tls-delegation.sh'`) and L1778 (`ci-cache-guard ... --stray-scan -- bash scripts/tests/test-tls-delegation.sh`); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1995 (same run line), L2008 (`rules: changes:` entry) and L2028 (second `changes:` entry); `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2502 and L2523 (trigger `paths:` entries) and L2549 (run line); `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L199; `scripts/tests/setup-lib-shim-tls.test.ts` L5 (comment "The unchanged oracle is scripts/tests/test-tls-delegation.sh": rewrite to name the new file, or keep if the Bash oracle is retained during the transition); `scripts/tests/setup-retarget-others.test.ts` L7 (header "Suites served: ... test-tls-delegation.sh (6)"); `scripts/tests/lib/setup-declarations-consumers.ts` L103 (data record `suite: "scripts/tests/test-tls-delegation.sh (r3 #11, #20)"`); specs 0256 L216 and L524 (in-process sourcing) and L701 ("the oracle `test-tls-delegation.sh` pins the shell format": this sentence makes the Bash suite an ORACLE for the writer format, so the retirement must first confirm `tls-env-writer.test.ts` pins that format, which it does at L66-L84), spec 0260 L73. The suite's own paths-filter coverage means the new `scripts/tests/tls-delegation-contract.test.ts` must be added to the same three CI path lists, and by explicit name to the `node --test` command of the owning job (capability `setup`): no glob covers `scripts/tests/*.test.ts`, and `check-test-wiring.ts` and `check-stray-scan-wiring.ts` police only `test-*.sh`, so an unlisted file never runs and nothing goes red.

#### Services (pull request D)

##### `scripts/tests/test-mcp-daemon.sh` (1577 lines)

- **What the Bash suite really does.** Sources `scripts/lib/common.sh` into one shell (L121) with `HOME` set to a `mktemp -d`, then runs 23 groups against shell functions. Only group G17 goes through a script (`bash scripts/status-mcp-server.sh`, a 22-line shim that guards Node and `exec`s `scripts/status-mcp-server.ts`). G2, G3, G19 execute the installed launcher, a copy of `scripts/lib/mcp-daemon-launcher.sh` materialised by `install_mcp_launcher`. Counts: about 122 `ok/nope` assertions. Two groups (G14 behavioural half, G21) are conditionally skipped when `mempalace` is not importable or ChromaDB is not on `127.0.0.1:8001`.
- **Existing TS suites that already overlap (do NOT duplicate).**
  - `status-mcp-lifecycle.test.ts` (and `-windows`): "no log at", "probe log line", "NOT ENFORCED", "DRIFTED", "VERIFIED", "USURPED", "UNVERIFIABLE" (covers G17 13a-13d). Not covered by literal: "LOCKED OUT" / "http (shared daemon)" (13e, 13f; the entry prints "locked out of writes by the daemon's exclusive lease", `status-report.ts` L186-206). `service-owner-check.test.ts`, `service-listener-pid.test.ts` pin the verdict logic.
  - `switch-transaction.test.ts` ("could not be switched", rollback order, partial-state NOTE at `switch-transaction.ts` L103) and `switch-replace.test.ts` ("squatter PID", "did not accept the current token") cover the TS twins of G15, G20.
  - `service-launcher-token.test.ts` ("bearer token file not found", "ChromaDB daemon unreachable") and `service-token.test.ts`, `service-token-argv.test.ts` pin the TS launcher-token and the argv rule; they do NOT run the Bash launcher.
  - `mcp-launcher-lifecycle.test.ts` (POSIX) runs the installed launcher under a restart model; it does not assert the token refusals of G2.
  - `service-unit-render.test.ts`, `setup-chroma-unit.test.ts` cover the TS unit renderer ("__UNKNOWN" placeholder refusal); they do NOT call `_materialise_mcp_unit` of `common.sh`.
  - `setup-declarations.test.ts`, `setup-retarget-others.test.ts` mention `ensure-mempalace-http`; verify at plan time whether the four-CLI order assertion of G13(b) is already there before writing it.
  - `service-launcher-palace-path.test.ts` (spec 0260) covers launcher text and child env, not the `mcp_token_path` symlink key (G1 case 4).
  - Nothing in `scripts/tests/*.test.ts` covers: `ensure_mempalace_home` (grep: 0 hits), `_transport_is_http` reaper guard, `MCP_RESERVED_NAMES`, the jq-argv leak (#1247), `register_mempalace_mcp`/`mcp_assistant_arrangement`/`capture`/`restore` of `common.sh` as Bash functions.

- **Replacement suite.** Group labels `G1`..`G23` are this inventory's numbering, in file order. Every file below is TypeScript, under 300 lines, `node:test`.

  All files except `mcp-daemon-static-guards.test.ts` and `mcp-daemon-status.test.ts` source Bash `common.sh` and are marked "J4 deletes with the library": `mcp-daemon-token-launcher`, `mcp-daemon-registration`, `mcp-daemon-switch`, `mcp-daemon-replace` and the Bash-function legs of the static file. Each carries a header comment saying so. Where a group's subject has a TypeScript twin already covered by an existing suite (G15, G17, G20 partially) the new test targets only the `common.sh` copy, so that J4's deletion of the library takes the test with it and leaves the TS suite as the lasting pin.

- **Isolation.**
  - Real HOME paths touched by the Bash suite if the guard were removed: `~/.crewrig/mcp-daemon-launcher.sh` (overridden by `MEMPALACE_MCP_LAUNCHER_PATH`), `~/.mempalace/palace`, `~/.mempalace/mcp-server.log`, the token file keyed from the palace path under `~/.mempalace/`, `~/.claude.json`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json`, `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json` (hence `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` all in the scratch directory; never `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot` of the operator). Assert in `before` that `os.homedir()` is not consulted: set `HOME` and `USERPROFILE` and also assert `path.resolve(process.env.HOME)` starts with the scratch root.
  - OS services: none loaded. `install_daemon_supervisor` would reach `launchctl` / `systemctl --user` keyed by uid, but the Bash suite never calls it (no plist exists for the randomised label; comment L1279-1289). `mcp_daemon_replace_process` takes its Darwin `else` branch with no unit loaded. The replacement keeps that, and sets `CREWRIG_TEST_SERVICE_BIN_DIR=<scratch>/svc-bin` holding stub `launchctl`, `systemctl`, `schtasks` that exit 1 and append their argv to a log, so any accidental supervisor call is caught by an assertion that the log is empty (it is a tripwire, `scripts/lib/service/exec.ts` L24, L100 honours it; the Bash `common.sh` path does not, so the stub dir is also prepended to `PATH`).
  - Network: ports on `127.0.0.1` only. Port axis: free port from `listen(0)`; `HERMETIC_PORT` = a second free port (never `port + 1`, which can be taken; the Bash note about TIME_WAIT on the main port is moot with in-process servers, which `close()` cleanly). The behavioural half (G14, G21) must still use the real `8001` ChromaDB and bind a real daemon on the free port; it is skipped when `python -c 'import mempalace'` fails or `http://127.0.0.1:8001/api/v2/heartbeat` is unreachable, with the same two-line reason printed through `t.skip(reason)`. Replace `pkill -f "transport http --host 127.0.0.1 --port N"` by killing the recorded pid (the double-fork pid is learned from the launcher's endpoint record or by `lsof`-free `netstat` of the free port); `pkill -f` by pattern is forbidden because a sibling test on another free port cannot match but an operator daemon on a coincident port could.
  - External commands: `jq`, `curl`, `shasum`/`sha256sum`, `stat`, `mktemp`, `python3`, `claude`/`gemini`/`copilot`/`agy` (stubbed). Required real: `bash`, `jq` (hard assertion in `before`, Node 24 floor, not a skip), `python3` (only for the launcher's interpreter substitution and G21). Stub `gh` is not used here.
  - Cleanup: `after()` removes the scratch root; stub servers `close()`d in `finally`.
