- **Changed or dropped assertions.**
  1. G4: the Bash `run_launcher_bounded` and its self-test pinned a Bash watchdog defect (#1426). The TS runner has no inherited-pipe hazard; the test keeps the same two properties (killed at the bound; fast path not taxed) on `runLauncherBounded`. Nothing about the launcher is lost.
  2. G6: self-sweep path `scripts/tests/test-mcp-daemon.sh` is replaced by the glob of the new test files and the rig, with a non-empty check (the guard must still read itself). `hooks/mempalace-transcript.sh` stays (shim of hook, row C3, still the carrier of the `-K -` curl config).
  3. G9, G10: `stat -c` / `stat -f` GNU-first dance replaced by `fs.statSync().mode & 0o777`; same property.
  4. G13: reads the TS printer `print-setup-declarations.ts`; keep as is (already retargeted in 0256 D1). If `setup-declarations.test.ts` already asserts the ordering, G13(b) becomes a pointer test, not a duplicate.
  5. G14, G21: `sleep`/`curl` loops replaced by `fetch` with `AbortSignal.timeout` polling; the 20 s survival window is kept as real time (cannot be shortened without weakening the reaper claim; mark the test `{ timeout: 90_000 }` and keep it conditional).
  6. G17 13a-13d: moved to the existing `status-mcp-lifecycle.test.ts` by reference (the Bash group duplicates it now that the shim is a row D shim); a cross-check test pins the literals. 13e, 13f are new TS tests.
  7. `fake-mcp.py` (Python `socketserver`) is replaced by an in-process Node server; the spec 0182 readiness requirement is met by `listen` callback plus a `/healthz` self-fetch (precondition test of 13b kept).
  8. `pkill -f` removed (see Isolation); lost nothing.
  9. Preflights: `jq` becomes a hard assertion in `before`; `mempalace` import and ChromaDB reachability remain skip conditions with reasons printed (environmental, same as Bash).
  10. Nothing dropped; the counts of `ok` assertions per group must be equal or higher.

- **Mutation set.** Mutate a temp copy of git-tracked files only (reuse `scripts/tests/lib/mutation-copy.ts`; never edit the worktree). Each must turn the named group red in BOTH the Bash suite (against the original, before it is deleted) and the new suite.
  1. `scripts/lib/common.sh` `mcp_token_path` (L2122-): replace `resolved="$(cd -P "$palace_path" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)"` by `resolved="$palace_path"` -> G1 (symlink case), G19.
  2. `scripts/lib/mcp-daemon-launcher.sh`: delete the length test `if [ "${#MEMPALACE_MCP_HTTP_TOKEN}" -lt 32 ]; then` block (L109-110) -> G2 (short token).
  3. Same file: change the charset test so `-` is rejected (drop `-` from the allowed class near L105) -> G3 (upstream-style token).
  4. `scripts/lib/common.sh` `register_mempalace_mcp` or `restore_mempalace_registration`: pass the token with `jq --arg token "$token"` instead of `$ENV.<VAR>` -> G23 (leak into jq argv).
  5. `scripts/lib/common.sh` L1714: move `applied="$applied $cli"` after the `register_mempalace_mcp` call -> G12 structural and G15 (claude not restored).
  6. `scripts/lib/common.sh` `ensure_mempalace_home` (L1085): delete the `mkdir -p "$home_dir/palace"` branch condition `[ -z "${MEMPALACE_PALACE_PATH:-}" ]` (always create palace) -> G22 override case; or remove the call from `install_mcp_daemon` before `install_daemon_supervisor` -> G22 structural.
  7. `scripts/lib/mempalace-http-wrapper.py` L108: change `if not _transport_is_http():` to `if True:` -> G14 static (and G21/G14 behavioural when run).
  8. `scripts/lib/common.sh` `_materialise_mcp_unit` (L1296): remove the unsubstituted-placeholder refusal -> G18.
  9. `scripts/lib/common.sh` `mcp_daemon_replace_process` (L1432-1435): drop the line printing `Evict any squatter process` -> G20 (R3 disclosure).
  10. `config/systemd/mempalace-mcp-server.service`: append `Environment=MEMPALACE_MCP_HTTP_TOKEN=abcdefgh12345678` -> G7. (Also `hooks/mempalace-transcript.sh`: reintroduce `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $t"` -> G6.)
  11. `scripts/status-mcp-server.sh` shim / `scripts/lib/service/status-report.ts` L196: drop the lockout branch -> G17 13e (new TS test only; Bash suite catches it through the shim).

- **Platform.** `mcp-daemon-token-launcher`, `mcp-daemon-registration`, `mcp-daemon-switch`, `mcp-daemon-replace`: POSIX-only (skip on `win32`: they source the Bash library and run the Bash launcher; the TypeScript twins already run on Windows in `service-windows.test.ts`, `status-mcp-lifecycle-windows.test.ts`, `service-launcher-token.test.ts`). `mcp-daemon-static-guards`: all platforms for G6, G7, G14 static, G22 structural (file reads only; use `fs.readFileSync` and avoid `awk`, port the awk range extraction to string scanning), POSIX-only for G13 if `print-setup-declarations.ts` spawns Bash (otherwise all). `mcp-daemon-status`: shim leg POSIX-only, entry leg all platforms. G14 behavioural and G21 additionally conditional on `mempalace` importable and ChromaDB on 8001.

Because this suite is deleted by J1b, the fix has two possible routes: (a) edit the comment in the same diff before deletion (pointless churn), or (b) carry the sentence into the new `mcp-daemon-static-guards.test.ts` G6 block comment with the corrected file name. Recommend (b); the guard comment must name `mempalace-transcript-hook.test.ts`. The comment names `test-mempalace-transcript-hook.sh` at `scripts/tests/test-mcp-daemon.sh` L367 (block L363-368); the new `mcp-daemon-static-guards.test.ts` G6 block comment carries the corrected name `mempalace-transcript-hook.test.ts`; no Bash edit (a comment-name carry-over, not a rename). Also the same file L462 names itself as a swept path (`scripts/tests/test-mcp-daemon.sh`), handled in Changed item 2.

##### `scripts/tests/test-chroma-server.sh` (355 lines)

- **What it does.** Section A (static, no daemon): text checks on `scripts/start-chroma-server.sh` and `scripts/lib/mempalace-http-wrapper.py`. Section B (behavioural): resolves the mempalace pipx venv with `common.sh` `detect_mempalace_python`, derives `<venv>/bin/chroma`; if either is missing SKIPs six checks; otherwise starts the REAL chroma daemon on port 18001 under a scratch HOME. L91-94 comment: the old Tests 9-11 (`ulimit -n`, `nohup` text) were already removed in spec 0252 R21 and live in `chroma-launch.test.ts`.
- **Overlap check.** `chroma-lifecycle.test.ts` (277 lines, entry + bash shim, stand-in `chroma` and loopback heartbeat via `lib/chroma-stand-in.ts`) already asserts: start idempotent same PID exit 0 (L93), stop removes PID file (L150), status NOT RUNNING exit 1 (L239). `chroma-launch.test.ts` L101 already asserts the start entry never contains `PersistentClient` (both the `.ts` and the shim, loop over files). `mempalace-http-client-reuse.test.ts` (321 lines) covers `_http_factory()` caching and that pool kwargs reach `HttpClient` with a FAKE chromadb package. NOT covered anywhere: wrapper source ordering (patch before `from mempalace`), the `Requires: chromadb>=` comment, the literal defaults 8/4, `settings=` at both HttpClient call sites, the daemon-unreachable stderr/exit with the real wrapper path, and the two checks against a REAL `chromadb` (`ClientAPI`, `http_limits`). Those are what the new files add.

The "skip six checks when the pipx venv is absent" behaviour is KEPT as `{ skip: "mempalace pipx venv not installed" }` on `chroma-wrapper-live.test.ts` only, with no sentinel test: the `chroma-mcp` job of `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` installs no pipx `mempalace` (the only `pip install` is `packaging` in job `mempalace`), so G7, G10 and G11 skip in CI with the printed reason and never fail under `CI`. G7 lives in `chroma-wrapper-live.test.ts` under the same skip as G10 and G11: `scripts/lib/mempalace-http-wrapper.py` runs the mempalace range guard (L232-245, exit 1 with a refusal text) and `import chromadb` (L248) before the unreachable probe (L317-325). No network beyond loopback.

##### `scripts/tests/test-chroma-health-race.sh` (180 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** Regression for #138: the health check at the tail of `install_daemon_supervisor` / `install_chroma_daemon` (`scripts/lib/common.sh` L961-985, marker `# Health check` at 2-space indent, terminator `^}$`) must poll with a 15 s budget (`deadline=$((SECONDS + 15))`, `sleep 0.3`) instead of one shot. Existing coverage: `scripts/tests/service-install.test.ts` already asserts the TS twin (`scripts/lib/service/install.ts`: 15 s poll in 0.3 s steps, shell error text, rollback hook; spec 0252 req 8-9). It does NOT execute the Bash block of `common.sh`, so the Bash copy would lose its only guard when this suite is removed; hence a bash-spawning port that disappears with `common.sh` in J4. Spec 0252 L478 places this suite in the "not touched until J4" class: see the SUMMARY ambiguity.

| replacement file | what it holds | est. lines |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/tests/chroma-health-race.test.ts` | extraction of the block from `common.sh`, behavioural run in a bash harness against a slow-start stub, static polling-construct check; uses `runCommon`-style bash spawning, with its own tiny harness (no rig needed) | ~110 |

- **Isolation.** The stub status script lives in a scratch `repo_dir`; the extracted block is run in a fresh `bash` with `health_fn=_stub_health` (no `status-chroma-server.sh` of the repository, no real daemon, no launchd/systemd, no port). No HOME access (set `HOME` and `USERPROFILE` to the scratch root anyway). No external commands but `bash` and `date`. The stub marker must use a monotonic or injected clock rather than `date +%s` seconds when feasible: in TS, create `started_at` as `Date.now()` ms in the file and have the stub compare to `$(node -e ...)`? That adds a Node dependency inside the stub; keep `date +%s` and a 2 s ready delay, so the test costs about 2-3 s wall clock (same as Bash). Tolerance: the Bash assertion has no upper time bound; keep that.

- **Assertion mapping.** Labels: G1 = L44-106 setup and extraction (a failure here is test 0), G2 = L108-152 behavioural, G3 = L154-173 static.

| label | behaviour asserted | replacement::test intent | MUST survive |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 (L46-50, L100-106) | `common.sh` exists; the awk extraction (`/^  # Health check/` starts capture, `/^}$/` ends it) yields a non-empty block; empty block is a failure naming the missing marker `# Health check` | health-race::extraction non-vacuity | the marker `# Health check` (two leading spaces) and the terminator `^}$`; non-empty block as a hard failure; the marker comment in `common.sh` L964-967 saying it is load-bearing must stay |
| G2 (L108-152) | harness: `health_check() { local repo_dir="$1"; local health_fn=_stub_health; local log_hint="(test)"; local label="(test)"; <BLOCK> }`, `_stub_health() { bash "${CREWRIG_REPO_DIR}/scripts/status-chroma-server.sh"; }`, `CREWRIG_REPO_DIR="$1"`; stub exits 2 if the marker file is missing, exits 1 with `stub: daemon not ready yet (elapsed=Ns)` while `now - started_at < 2`, then prints `stub: daemon healthy (elapsed=Ns)` and exits 0; the block must return 0 against a daemon healthy after 2 s | health-race::block survives a 2 s slow start (rc 0) | the block is wrapped in a function so `return N` behaves as in the real function; the three locals `health_fn`, `log_hint`, `label`; slow start of 2 s; assertion rc == 0; failure message includes the combined output |
| G3 (L154-173) | the block text matches `(\bwhile\b\|\buntil\b\|\bfor\b\|deadline=\|SECONDS)` (a retry construct) so a single-shot regression cannot pass G2 on a fast machine | health-race::block contains a polling construct | the five alternatives; extended `grep -E` semantics (`\b` word boundary in TS: `/\b(while\|until\|for)\b\|deadline=\|SECONDS/`) |

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** The Bash header's "Expected behaviour: against current main FAIL" prose is dropped (historical). The harness is built in memory with `spawnSync("bash", ["-c", ...])` instead of a temp `.sh`, removing the `mktemp -t ... .sh` template difference between BSD and GNU. Nothing dropped. Optional tightening (not in the Bash suite): assert the poll budget `deadline=$((SECONDS + 15))` and `sleep 0.3` literals; defer to `service-install.test.ts` for the TS twin and do NOT add here unless the spec author wants parity (it would couple the test to wording).
- **Mutation set.**
  1. `scripts/lib/common.sh` L968-976: replace the `while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do ... done` loop by a single `"$health_fn" >/dev/null 2>&1 && healthy=1` -> G2 (rc 1) and G3 (no construct).
  2. same file: change `deadline=$((SECONDS + 15))` to `deadline=$((SECONDS + 1))` -> G2 (budget shorter than the 2 s start).
  3. same file: rename the marker `# Health check` to `# Health probe` -> G1 (empty block).
  4. same file: change the function's closing `}` indentation (e.g. `}`) so the terminator is not found -> G1/G2 (block spans to EOF or empty).
  5. same file: replace `sleep 0.3` by `sleep 30` -> G2 (timing; documented only: it would make the test slow rather than red, so cap the harness with a 20 s `timeout` and assert completion).
  6. same file: make the loop `return 0` unconditionally -> G2 stays green (documents a blind spot: the Bash suite cannot tell a block that never fails; if the spec author wants it, add a second harness where the stub never becomes healthy and assert rc 1 and the text `did not become healthy`; propose as an additive test, flagged "new, not in Bash").
- **Platform.** POSIX-only (skip on `win32`: the subject is a Bash block of `common.sh` and the stub is a Bash script; the Windows twin is `chroma-windows-*.test.ts`, `service-windows.test.ts`).
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1633 (stray-scan step `Test Chroma Health Race`) and the path-filter / `hashFiles` glob `scripts/tests/test-chroma-*.sh` at L1627; `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1893; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2432; `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L145; comment in `scripts/lib/common.sh` L965 ("scripts/tests/test-chroma-health-race.sh extracts the block between it and this function's closing brace ... rewording it breaks that regression test") MUST be updated to name `scripts/tests/chroma-health-race.test.ts` (comment-only, in a core-layer file, not a row-J-protected edit; no `artifacts/` change so no build); the test's own header at L2; spec 0252 L478-481 (history).
- **Wiring-wide notes for all three suites.** (1) When the last `test-chroma-*.sh` that survives is `test-chroma-server.sh` (355 lines, not in this fragment), the glob in the three CI configs still matches something; if a later row retires it, the glob must be replaced, not left dangling (spec 0260 L275 describes the same trap for `test-mempalace-*.sh`). (2) Each new `.test.ts` must be listed wherever the CI lists test files for the `chroma-mcp` filter (GitHub `build.yml` L1590-1600 filter `chroma-mcp`, GitLab `changes:` L1865-1925, `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2365-2414), otherwise edits to them will not trigger the job. (3) `check-test-wiring` governance (`governance-check-test-wiring.test.ts`) and `check-test-strays` must accept the new files; run both when planning. (4) `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L144, L145, L179 are removed in the same diff as the three Bash files.

##### `scripts/tests/test-chroma-fd-limits.sh` (109 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** Static config assertions, no daemon. No existing TS suite pins the 65536 floor: `chroma-launch.test.ts` pins the soft limit raised by the start entry (spec 0087), `setup-chroma-unit.test.ts` and `service-unit-render.test.ts` render units but do not assert `NumberOfFiles` / `LimitNOFILE` in the shipped templates (grep: only the Bash suite and the config files contain `LimitNOFILE`/`NumberOfFiles`). Spec 0252 L221 forbids editing `config/launchd/` and `config/systemd/`; this test only reads them.

| `scripts/tests/chroma-fd-limits.test.ts` | the three groups, `fs.readFileSync` of the two chroma unit files; no helper | ~70 |

| label | behaviour asserted | replacement::test intent | MUST survive |
|---|---|---|---|

| G1b (L72-77) | same for `HardResourceLimits` | plist::hard limit >= floor | same; the key text `HardResourceLimits` |
| G1c (L79-86) | hard >= soft (R2: the soft limit is enforceable); both must be present | plist::hard >= soft | comparison `hard >= soft`; present-ness of both |
| G2 (L92-102) | `config/systemd/mempalace-chroma-server.service` exists; the LAST line matching `^[[:space:]]*LimitNOFILE=<n>` (`tail -n1`) is >= 65536 | systemd::LimitNOFILE >= floor | last-match semantics (a later override wins); the file exists (today L12 `LimitNOFILE=65536`) |
| G3 (L104-109) | exit status 1 when any assertion failed | implicit in `node --test` (non-zero on failure); no replacement test | none beyond the runner's status |

Spec author may optionally extend the same floor to `config/launchd/com.mempalace.mcp-server.plist` and `mempalace-mcp-server.service`; the Bash suite does not, so that is out of scope here and flagged as an open question only.

  4. `config/systemd/mempalace-chroma-server.service` L12: `LimitNOFILE=65536` -> `LimitNOFILE=4096` -> G2.
  5. same file: append a second line `LimitNOFILE=1024` after the first -> G2 (last wins).
  6. same file: delete the `LimitNOFILE=` line -> G2.
- **Platform.** All platforms (pure file reads; use `\n` and `\r?\n` tolerant regexes since checkout on Windows may be CRLF).
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1630 (stray-scan step `Test Chroma Fd Limits`, L1628-1631, `if: steps.filter.outputs.chroma-mcp == 'true'`); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1892; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2431; `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L144; glob `scripts/tests/test-chroma-*.sh` in `build.yml` L1627 (still matches `test-chroma-server.sh`; add `scripts/tests/chroma-fd-limits.test.ts` to the path filter, key-files and `hashFiles`, or the paths trigger will stop firing for edits to it); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1892-1897 key-files, `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2371-2414 path lists; spec 0087 L97 and spec 0252 L221 (history). - **Changed or dropped assertions.** `mode_of` GNU-then-BSD probing becomes `fs.statSync`; the "never use `stat -f` first" pitfall disappears. `sleep` is not used. `$HOME`-only isolation becomes HOME + USERPROFILE + `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set to the scratch dir (the Bash header warns that `common.sh` may one day honour `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; the TS tests must set it so the guarantee holds for the TS entry, which `repair-verbs.test.ts` L1-12 already does for its fixture). The ACL builder (`chmod +a` on macOS, `setfacl`, `sudo -n chown`) is kept as three helper branches with explicit skip reasons, never silent passes. Nothing else dropped.
- **Mutation set** (temp copy of git-tracked files; old and new suites must both fail): (1) `scripts/lib/service/repair-backup.ts`: pick the first parseable backup instead of the lexically greatest -> G2, G3. (2) same file: skip the `.NN` handling (sort by stamp only) -> G3. (3) `scripts/lib/service/repair.ts`/`repair-backup.ts`: replace the staged-temp-then-rename restore with `fs.copyFileSync(bak, cfg)` (writes through a symlink) -> G6. (4) restore mode: read mode with `lstatSync` instead of `statSync` -> G7. (5) drop the token-bearing `0o600` clamp -> G4, G8. (6) owner-read test of the mode filter: accept `100` (owner triad non-zero rather than 4-7) -> G10; drop the width check -> G9. (7) `repair-backup.ts` L89: change `NO USABLE BACKUP` text -> G5. (8) remove `--` in `switch-rollback.ts` L56 (`-- --restore-backup`) -> G16; separate text mutant on `switch-transaction.ts` L94 (`task mempalace:repair`, R14 tail). `switch-transaction.ts` L94 contains no `--`. (9) arg parser: make `--restore-backup --reset-none` non-exclusive -> G17. (10) `scripts/repair-mempalace-http.sh`: `exec node` -> `node` (exit status lost) -> G1 exit codes.
- **Platform.** `repair-restore-modes.test.ts`: POSIX-only (skip on win32: POSIX modes, symlinks, umask, bash shim; `repair-windows.test.ts` already covers the Windows entry). `repair-report-handoff.test.ts`: report tests all platforms through the `.ts` entry; the sourced-`common.sh` parts POSIX-only.
- **Isolation.** The Bash suite touches only `$HOME` (config paths from `mcp_assistant_config_path`: `~/.claude.json`, `~/.gemini/settings.json`, `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json`, `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json`); no launchd, systemd, schtasks, port or network. Replacement: `mkdtemp` HOME and USERPROFILE, refuse to run when it equals `os.homedir()` (use `scripts/tests/lib/real-home-guard.ts`), PATH stub dir with the four assistants, no `gh`.
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1598, L1599 (path triggers), L1627, L1630-L1645 (cache-guard key files list); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1876, L1877, L1892-L1897, L1904, L1905, L1921, L1922; `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2373, L2374, L2391, L2392, L2416, L2417, L2435 (`ci-cache-guard --stray-scan -- bash scripts/tests/test-repair-mempalace-http.sh`: replace by the TS files), `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L191 (plus L193 for rotate); `specs/0187-linux-owner-read-ci-coverage.md` L19-L47 (names the suite as the subject of the Linux owner-read fixture requirement: the new file must keep those requirements and the spec must cite the TS file); `specs/0260-hook-ops-tests-typescript.md` L74 (assigns to J1b).

Spec 0176 is the origin (header L2).

#### MemPalace doctor and pins (pull request E)

##### `scripts/tests/test-mempalace-doctor.sh` (1358 lines)

- **What the Bash suite does.** Fabricates a machine per scenario: scratch HOME with four CLI MCP registration files (`.claude.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `.copilot/mcp-config.json`, `.gemini/config/mcp_config.json`), a fixture `PATH` of fake `mempalace`/`mempalace-mcp` console scripts, fake checkouts (copies of the wrapper, `common.sh`, `mempalace_pin.py`), and "interpreters" that are `#!/bin/sh` shims doing `exec env PYTHONPATH=<fake site> python3 "$@"` over a fake `mempalace-<v>.dist-info/METADATA` (the doctor only READS a console script's shebang, never execs it). It runs `bash scripts/doctor-mempalace.sh` and greps the report. Report text now comes from `scripts/lib/service/doctor-*.ts` (strings below were verified there).
- **Overlap check (ls scripts/tests, grep).** `doctor-sections.test.ts` (110 lines, entry + shim, isolated HOME, three headings, NOT PRESENT, UNGUARDED, MALFORMED, WRAPPER MISSING for a nonexistent path, HTTP registration) and `doctor-sections-windows.test.ts` (Windows fourth section) already cover section structure; they do NOT build fake interpreters, so version/pin/divergence/fallback/lockout/shell-wrapper behaviours are uncovered. `mempalace-python.test.ts`, `mempalace-python-shebang.test.ts`, `mempalace-pin.test.ts` pin the TS twins at unit level (pipx home order, polyglot shebang forms incl. apostrophe escape, `$`/backtick rejection, sibling fallback, "a shell is never returned"); they do NOT assert the doctor's report lines. The replacement therefore keeps the end-to-end report assertions and does not re-test the unit forms. `setup-retarget-others.test.ts` L134 ("the launcher path per CLI") already pins the Bash G9 declaration reads (`mcp.wrapper-script`, `mcp.wrapper-carrier`). The new tests MUST NOT reuse `lib/doctor-fixture.ts` for the machine (it builds only empty HOMEs); they add `lib/doctor-machine.ts` and import `isolatedEnv` ideas only.
- **Group inventory (file order).** G1 = L329-444 scenario 1 (divergent installs, argv shapes, labels, R10 fallback); G2 = L446-506 clean machine; G3 = L508-539 absent surfaces; G4 = L541-582 dist-info/`__version__` disagreement; G5 = L584-637 range-only failure plus env-shebang; G6 = L639-717 divergence-only failure; G7 = L719-763 missing wrapper; G8 = L765-842 NO VERSION; G9 = L844-941 R11 launch paths plus pin-literal scan; G10 = L943-959 mutates nothing; G11 = L961-1000 lockout; G12 = L1002-1353 shell-wrapper console scripts (12a-12l).

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** (1) jq is no longer needed: JSON is written with `JSON.stringify`; `jq` preflight and the `FATAL jq is required` exit are dropped, `jq` removed from the toolbin list (the TS doctor never calls it); `node` stays in it. (2) `FATAL`/exit-2 preflights (`python3` interpreter, readable pin in `common.sh`) become hard assertions in a `before` hook (a missing interpreter FAILS, never skips; `CREWRIG_TEST_PYTHON` override kept). (3) G9 pin-literal scan retargeted from the shim to the TS sources (see table). (4) G11 replaces the python `http.server` + `sleep 1` with a `node:http` server on a free port; `MEMPALACE_MCP_PORT` random 19000-19899 becomes an OS-assigned free port. (5) `sed -i.bak` pin edit becomes a JS string replace on the copied `common.sh`. (6) G9 `check_declared_launcher` calls through `node print-setup-declarations.ts`; replacement imports the declarations module directly (or spawns the same file) and keeps the vacuity guard. (7) The header comment's references to the doctor "being a Bash script" are stale: the doctor is the TS entry behind a shim, and G1-G12 run through the SHIM (`bash scripts/doctor-mempalace.sh`) so the shim, the floor guard and the entry are all exercised; entry-direct parity is already in `doctor-sections.test.ts`. Nothing else dropped.
- **Isolation.** Real paths avoided: `~/.claude.json`, `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot`, `~/.local/pipx`, `~/Library/Application Support/pipx`, `~/.crewrig`: HOME and USERPROFILE point at a scratch dir per scenario (the doctor derives every one of those from HOME). `PATH` is REPLACED (not prepended) for G3, G5-G8, G12 so the machine's real `mempalace`/`mempalace-mcp`/`python3` cannot answer; for G1, G2, G4, G10, G11 it is prepended as in Bash (assert nothing about versions that the real machine could supply, or move them to the isolated runner). `PIPX_HOME`, `XDG_DATA_HOME` and all `MEMPALACE_*` are deleted from the child env unless the case sets them. Network: the doctor probes the daemon on `MEMPALACE_MCP_PORT`; always set to a free port (G11: a `node:http` server there, closed in `after`; other groups: a port nothing listens on, e.g. a port allocated then closed). OS services: none on POSIX (the fourth section is Windows-only, spec 0252); still set `CREWRIG_TEST_SERVICE_BIN_DIR` to an empty dir so no stub or real `launchctl`/`systemctl`/`schtasks` can be reached. External commands: the interpreter shims need `sh`, `env` and an ABSOLUTE python (never the relative `CREWRIG_TEST_PYTHON`, which would loop on a shim named `python3`). Add the `real-home-guard.ts` `realHomeGuard()` assertion in `after` of each file.

##### `scripts/tests/test-mempalace-runtime-guard.sh` (796 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** Black-box: the real wrapper is run end to end as a `python3` subprocess against a fabricated interpreter environment (fake `mempalace-<v>.dist-info/METADATA`, stub `mempalace/` and `chromadb/` packages on PYTHONPATH; per-case fixture tree holding COPIES of the committed wrapper, `mempalace_pin.py` and `common.sh`). Overlap check: `scripts/tests/mempalace-http-client-reuse.test.ts` (spec 0242, 321 lines) runs the same wrapper with a similar fixture pattern but asserts the HTTP client singleton only and uses the real `lib/` paths; it asserts none of the R3/R4 refusal fields, Phase A/B, the fallback comparator, R2 or sys.path neutrality, so nothing here duplicates it. No other `.test.ts` runs the wrapper. `setup-mempalace-version.test.ts` and `mempalace-python.test.ts` cover setup-side version detection, not the launch guard. The fixture builder is NOT shared with `mempalace-http-client-reuse.test.ts` (that file keeps its own; folding it in is out of scope and would touch a shipped test).
- Shared helper note: the Bash fixture authors no pin literal; the TS port reads the bounds from `common.sh` with an independent two-line regex (`^MEMPALACE_MIN_VERSION="([^"]*)"$` and the MAX counterpart, multiline), not via `scripts/lib/mempalace-pin.ts`, so the guard test keeps its independence from the TS reader.

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** `sed`-built fixtures become JS string transforms. The `printf`/`sed` indentation of driver output is dropped (driver output is attached to the assertion message instead). The "SKIPPED (not counted)" echo blocks (L731-735, L751-755, L782-784) have no analogue: in TS an absent packaging interpreter is ONE failing test (`G4c`) with the install hint, and the dependent tests are marked `skip` with reason (node:test counts them as skipped, not passed; the Bash summary-count difference between machines is thereby preserved). Nothing dropped.
- **Isolation.** Only `python3` subprocesses, no network, no port, no service manager, no `gh`. `common.sh` is read from the repo and never written; all derived fixtures go into one `mkdtempSync` root per file. HOME and USERPROFILE point at a scratch dir (the detector fallback sources `common.sh`, which can look under `~/.local/pipx`; the scratch HOME makes that find nothing, so the real-packaging path relies on `CREWRIG_TEST_PYTHON` or the ambient `python3`, as in CI after `pip install packaging`). The real `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot` and `~/.crewrig` are never read or written. `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` on every spawn so the repo `scripts/lib/__pycache__` is not created.
- **Mutation set.**
  1. `scripts/lib/mempalace_pin.py` `check()` (L112-130): replace the lower bound compare with `Version("0")` -> G4a/G4b/G4c "immediately below the floor" and "far below the floor" rows red (the issue-#623 hardcoded-floor mutant, named in the Bash header).
  2. same function: make the upper bound inclusive (`<=` for `<`) -> G4 "ceiling exactly (EXCLUSIVE)" row red, G3 `<max>` row red.
  3. `_FALLBACK_RE` (L75): loosen to accept `-git` or `-nightly` (e.g. `[-.+a-z0-9]*`) -> G3 regression rows and G5 Arm A grammar violation red; Arm B red when packaging refuses.
  4. `_FALLBACK_RE`: accept an epoch `1!` -> G3 `1!<min>` row red, G5 Arm A ordering violation red.
  5. `read_pin` (L85-110): drop the duplicate-declaration check -> G2 "duplicated" red.
  6. `read_pin`: drop the `export`/unquoted rejection (relax `_PIN_RE` L46) -> G2 "export-prefixed" and "unquoted value" red.
  7. add a module-scope `print("x")` before `if __name__ == "__main__":` (L180) -> G7 stray-print red.
  8. add `import packaging` at module scope -> G7 third-party-import red (and the module no longer imports on a packaging-less interpreter).
  9. replace the `InvalidVersion` handler in `check()` so it returns `True` -> G6 red (both provider and real).
  10. add the literal floor string as a comment in `mempalace_pin.py` -> G1 R5 red.
  11. `COMPARATOR_FALLBACK` (L39) renamed -> G3 and G5 label asserts red.
- **Platform.** All three files: all platforms, python subprocess only. `findPackagingPython()` fallback via `bash` detector is POSIX-only (on win32 it returns none unless `CREWRIG_TEST_PYTHON` carries `packaging`, which CI Windows jobs must provide; if no Windows job runs this suite, mark G4c/G5 Arm B/G6-real `skip` on win32 with reason "real packaging interpreter resolution is POSIX-only"). No file requires Bash otherwise.
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1526 (step `Test Mempalace Pin`; the preceding step L1523-1524 `python3 -m pip install --quiet packaging` MUST stay, it is the reason G4c passes in CI; key-files glob to extend with `scripts/tests/mempalace-pin-*.test.ts,scripts/tests/lib/mempalace-pin-rig.ts,scripts/tests/fixtures/mempalace-pin/**`); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1741 (same; its pip install of packaging is on the sibling line, verify when editing); `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2290 (comment naming `test-mempalace-pin.sh` as failing loudly without an interpreter importing `packaging`: reword) and L2295 (command), pip install line L2294 stays; `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L170 (delete); in-suite self references L2, L75, L119, L139, L362, L536 (vanish with the file; the fixture copies must be reworded). `scripts/lib/mempalace_pin.py` docstring/comments naming the suite: none found by grep. `scripts/tests/mempalace-pin.test.ts` does not name this suite. No hit in `Taskfile.yml`, `docs/`, `artifacts/`.

##### `scripts/tests/test-mempalace-version-range.sh` (212 lines)

- **Replacement suite.** The scan is not a product script: `scan_repo` (L63-92) lives inside the suite, so the replacement carries the scan as a test library, `scripts/tests/lib/mempalace-range-scan.ts`, plus one test file. Overlap check: `scripts/tests/mempalace-pin.test.ts` L107-119 (`installSpec`) tests the TS builder of the install spec string, NOT the repo-wide literal sweep; `setup-mempalace-version.test.ts` and `setup-twins-differential-version.test.ts` cover setup-side detection. Nothing duplicates the sweep, and no other suite reads docs/Taskfile for pin literals. The `scan_repo` semantics are preserved on `git grep` (not a JS tree walk), so only tracked files are scanned and the same pathspec excludes apply.
- Pin literals: the Bash suite hard-codes `NEW_MIN="3.6.0"`, `NEW_MAX="3.7"` (L46-47) and the superseded `OLD_MIN="3.3.3"`, `OLD_MAX="3.4"`. Decision for the spec: the replacement derives `NEW_MIN`/`NEW_MAX` from `common.sh` (via the independent regex of `lib/mempalace-guard-fixture.ts`'s `readPin`) so a pin bump needs one edit; the superseded range stays assembled from parts. This is a change from the Bash suite (flagged below).

| replacement file | what it holds | est. lines |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/tests/lib/mempalace-range-scan.ts` (new) | `scanRepo(repoDir, {expectedSpec, oldLiteralRe})` returning `{violations: string[]}` with the two `git grep` passes and the per-token compare; `EXCLUDES` constant (`:(exclude)docs/adr/**`, `:(exclude)specs/**`, the two self paths below); `newRepo()/makeCommit()` helpers for hermetic git-init fixtures | ~80 |
| `scripts/tests/mempalace-version-range.test.ts` | G1..G6 | ~120 |

Group labels `G1`..`G6`: G1 = case a L132-141, G2 = case b L143-152, G3 = case c L154-163, G4 = case d L165-177, G5 = case e L179-193, G6 = real-repo enforcement L195-204. (Scan logic L61-92 and helpers L97-126 have no assertion of their own.)

- **Assertion mapping.**

| label | behaviour asserted | replacement::test intent | MUST survive |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | committed `README.md` containing `pipx install 'mempalace>=3.3.3,<3.4'` (assembled from parts): scan returns violations (check a, `STALE RANGE`) | range::superseded literal flagged | literal assembled at runtime (no contiguous `3.3.3,<3.4` in the new files; the new files are also excluded, see wiring) |
| G2 | `docs/x.md` with `run pipx install 'mempalace>=3.5.0,<3.6' now`: flagged (check b, `DIVERGENT RANGE`, token `mempalace>=3.5.0,<3.6`) | range::divergent numeric range flagged | the token grammar `mempalace>=[0-9][0-9.,<]*` |
| G3 | `docs/x.md` with `run pipx install '<EXPECTED_SPEC>' now` (current pin): clean | range::current pin passes | exact string `mempalace>=<min>,<max>` |
| G4 | `scripts/x.sh` with `pipx install "mempalace>=${MEMPALACE_MIN_VERSION},<${MEMPALACE_MAX_VERSION_EXCLUSIVE}"` (single-quoted in the test source so the placeholder stays literal): clean (anchor `mempalace>=[0-9]` must not match `$`) | range::interpolated form not flagged | `${...}` kept literal in a TS non-template string |
| G5 | superseded literal inside `docs/adr/0001-x.md` and `specs/0082.md` (both committed): clean (exempt trees) | range::exempt trees | both exempt roots in one commit |
| G6 | the real repository, scanned with the same `scanRepo`, has zero violations (violations printed with `file:line: 'token' (want '...')`) | range::real repo clean | runs against the real repo's tracked tree via `git -C <REPO> grep`; `CREWRIG_REPO_DIR` override honoured |

- **Changed or dropped assertions.** (1) `ok`/`ko` counters and "Results: N/M" line dropped for `node --test`. (2) Pin literals `3.6.0`/`3.7` derived from `common.sh` instead of hard-coded (see above); the divergent fixture `3.5.0,<3.6` stays a literal and MUST be asserted `!== expectedSpec` at test start so a future pin never makes it equal. (3) `EXCLUDES` names the suite file itself (L58); it now excludes `scripts/tests/mempalace-version-range.test.ts` and `scripts/tests/lib/mempalace-range-scan.ts`, and also the other replacement files that assemble the pin text at runtime (`scripts/tests/mempalace-runtime-guard-*.test.ts`, `scripts/tests/lib/mempalace-guard-fixture.ts`, `scripts/tests/mempalace-pin-*.test.ts`) ONLY IF a grep of the new files finds a numeric `mempalace>=<digit>` literal (they should not: use template strings with interpolation, so the sweep itself needs no extra excludes; verify with the real-repo test G6). Existing TypeScript tests already carry `mempalace>=3.6.0,<3.7` (`mempalace-pin.test.ts` L109, `prune-transcripts-ts-run.test.ts` L119, L167, L197, `prune-transcripts-ts.test.ts` L15); `scanRepo` scope and `EXCLUDES` are the Bash suite's at the base, ported unchanged. (4) The three hard git config lines (`user.email`, `user.name`, `commit.gpgsign`) become per-command `-c` options plus `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`/`GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` set to the null device so the dev machine's gitconfig cannot affect fixtures.
- **Isolation.** Temp git repos under `fs.mkdtempSync`, HOME and USERPROFILE set to a scratch dir, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`; the real repo is only READ by `git -C <REPO> grep` (G6). No network, port, service manager, `gh`, or CLI home path. Requires `git` on PATH (hard failure in `before` if absent).
- **Mutation set** (the G6 mutants edit a temp copy of the tracked tree: `git ls-files | copy` into a scratch git repo, edit, commit, call `scanRepo` on it; the scan library mutants are applied to a temp copy of `lib/mempalace-range-scan.ts` imported through a file URL).
  1. `Taskfile.yml` L31: change `'mempalace>=3.6.0,<3.7'` to `'mempalace>=3.6.0,<3.8'` -> G6 (divergent range) red.
  2. `README.md` (or any live doc): append the superseded literal `mempalace>=3.3.3,<3.4` -> G6 red (stale range).
  3. `docs/cli-matrix.md`: add a line outside `docs/adr/**` and `specs/**` carrying a divergent range (e.g. `pipx install 'mempalace>=3.5.0,<3.6'`) -> G6 red.
  4. scan library: drop the `docs/adr/**` exclude -> G5 red.
  5. scan library: change the anchor `mempalace>=[0-9]` to `mempalace>=` -> G4 red (interpolated form flagged).
  6. scan library: make check (b) compare with `!==` replaced by `===` (or skip the token loop) -> G2 and G3 red.
  7. scan library: delete check (a) pass -> G1 red.
  8. scan library: use `git grep --untracked` or a filesystem walk -> G1..G5 stay green but a new test "uncommitted fixture file is not scanned" would be needed; recommended extra test (not in the Bash suite): a file written but not `git add`ed is ignored (preserves the "tracked content only" contract). Mark as an addition in the spec, not a requirement of the Bash port.
- **Platform.** All platforms (git plus Node only). On win32 pathspec quoting `:(exclude)docs/adr/**` is passed as argv, no shell involved, so no skip.
- **Static reads and wiring.** `.github/workflows/build.yml` L1520 (step `Test Mempalace Version Range`; key-files glob `scripts/tests/test-mempalace-*.sh` to extend; this sweep is itself driven by the glob listed in the same line, so after migration no `test-mempalace-version-range.sh` token remains there); `.gitlab-ci.yml` L1739 (same); `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` L2286 (command); `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` L183 (delete); `scripts/lib/common.sh` L10 (comment: "kept in sync by scripts/tests/test-mempalace-version-range.sh", reword to the new test; comment-only edit to a core file, so `.crewrig/core-paths.txt` is unaffected but a reviewer should confirm no version-bump rule is triggered); `specs/0108-mempalace-runtime-version-guard.md` L157 (names the suite; specs are excluded from the sweep and immutable history, leave or handle in a delta-spec); self references L2, L32, L58 (vanish). `specs/0260-hook-ops-tests-typescript.md` L74. No hit in `Taskfile.yml` (apart from the literal pin at L31, which is the scan's subject), `docs/` or `artifacts/` for the suite name.

### Decision record

#### Pull request grouping

Five pull requests after #1614, so that each stays reviewable (about 2.5 to 4 thousand removed Bash
lines) and so that the long CI jobs overlap. They open from the same release tip in parallel
worktrees, are independent of one another except for the shared helpers named in the sections (a
helper is created by the first pull request that needs it and imported by later ones only after it has
merged), and merge one at a time at the coordinator's request. Because `ci/ci-capabilities.yml` and the
generated files are shared by every row of the wave, each pull request regenerates them from the current
release tip just before its merge request.

| PR | Branch | Suites | Bash lines removed |
|---|---|---|---:|
| 0 | `test/1614-decouple-setup-goldens-from-lock` | none (issue #1614, PR #1649) | 0 |
| A | `test/1341-setup-helpers` | the five suites of *Setup suites that source `common.sh`*, `test-setup-mcp-merge`, `test-setup-mcp-fold-secrets-argv`, `test-setup-ensure-tier-built` | about 2.4 k |
| B | `test/1341-setup-transcripts-optin` | the four `test-setup-*-transcript`, `test-setup-usage-capture-optin` | about 3.4 k |
| C | `test/1341-install-manage-components` | `test-component-tier-resolution`, `test-install-*` (2), `test-antigravity-component-install`, `test-system-context-store`, `test-setup-gemini-settings-merge`, `test-tls-delegation` | about 4.0 k |
| D | `test/1341-services` | `test-mcp-daemon`, `test-chroma-*` (3), `test-repair-mempalace-http`, `test-rotate-mempalace-token` | about 3.3 k |
| E | `chore/1341-mempalace-doctor-pins` | `test-mempalace-doctor`, `-runtime-guard`, `-pin`, `-version-range`; flips this spec and its deltas to `implemented`; documentation sweep | about 3.2 k |

A is first because it is small and settles the shape of the CI rewiring and the `bashLibs` port helper
that B to D reuse; PR C is the largest and the one most exposed to the static-read suites of
requirement 20, so it carries its retargeting commits first.

#### Rejected alternatives

- **One pull request per suite (thirty).** Rejected: the CI restart after each merge into the release
  branch costs 25 to 35 minutes per queued pull request, and the seat passes multiply.
- **One pull request for the whole row.** Rejected: about 17 thousand removed lines in one diff cannot
  be reviewed against the mapping.
- **Normalise the lock hash in the golden capture instead of a fixture lockfile.** Rejected, see requirement 13.
- **Leave the suites that source `common.sh` for J4.** Rejected: spec 0252 (L478-481) says the sourced
  suites are untouched until J4, but spec 0260 (L72-76) assigns them to J1b by name, and parent requirement
  13 provides the sourced-library exception that this row uses (requirement 6); leaving them would keep
  thirty entries in `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` until the last row of the epic.
- **Port the nested `node --test` wrappers and the copilot jq replay one for one.** Rejected: they assert
  the Bash file's own helpers, not the product.

## Scenarios

**Scenario:** A lockfile change no longer breaks the setup goldens

Given the setup sandbox with the fixture lockfile and a `package-lock.json` at the repository root that has
been altered
When the four setup golden suites run in an Ubuntu 24.04 container
Then every case passes.

**Scenario:** A broken setup merge turns the new suite red

Given the replacement of `test-setup-gemini-settings-merge.sh` and a temporary copy of
`scripts/lib/setup/gemini-settings*.ts` in which a merge step is mutated
When the replacement suite runs against the mutated copy
Then at least the test that the Bash suite named for that step fails, and the unmutated copy passes the
whole suite.

**Scenario:** A service suite never touches the real machine

Given a replacement of a chroma or MCP daemon suite
When it runs with a stub service directory, a scratch home and a free port
Then no LaunchAgent, systemd unit or scheduled task exists afterwards and the real `~/.claude`,
`~/.gemini`, `~/.copilot` and `~/.crewrig` are byte-identical to before.

**Scenario:** A sourced-library suite is retired with a substitute

Given the deletion of a Bash suite that sourced a `common.sh` function
When each assertion of the suite is looked up in its mapping
Then it names either an existing black-box test of the TypeScript twin or a bash-spawning port marked
`Retires with scripts/lib/common.sh (row J4)`.

**Scenario:** A missing prerequisite fails in CI

Given a CI runner without `packaging` for Python and with `CI` set
When the pin suite runs
Then it fails with a message naming the missing capability instead of skipping.

**Scenario:** A deleted suite leaves the repository consistent

Given a pull request that replaces a Bash suite
When `check-test-wiring`, `check-test-strays`, the shell ratchet and the CI generator run
Then they pass, `ci/shell-allowlist.txt` no longer lists the suite and no workflow, document or allowlist of
this row names it.

**Scenario:** A defect is found while porting

Given a replacement test that exposes a behaviour of a script that looks wrong
When the developer reaches that test
Then the behaviour is reported to the coordinator, the test asserts the current behaviour with a comment
naming the report, and the script is unchanged.

## Out of scope

- The Bash tests of rows J1a, J2 and J3 listed in requirement 2, and any edit to them.
- Retiring `scripts/lib/common.sh` and the other shell libraries, and the final shim removal (row J4).
- Changing the behaviour of a script, a hook, a fixture format or a diagnostic line.
- #1599, #1545, #1601, #1611, J2 (#1343) and the Windows timing-budget calibration (#1509).
- A change to `package-lock.json`.
- Any new dependency.

## Open questions

- **Sourced-library reading (requirement 6)** is confirmed with the coordinator before pull request A opens.
- **Chroma live checks** (`test-chroma-server.sh` G7, G10, G11) need a pipx `mempalace` environment with
  `chroma`; the PLAN decides whether the CI job provides it (fail under `CI`) or the checks stay skipped
  with a stated reason.
- **Pin literals** of `test-mempalace-version-range.sh`: the PLAN decides whether the replacement derives
  `NEW_MIN` and `NEW_MAX` from `common.sh` instead of hard-coding `3.6.0` and `3.7`.
- **Per-suite VERIFY items** carried in the *Suite sections* are resolved in the PLAN before any pull
  request opens (requirement 21 gates every pull request on them, not only pull request A).
